#+TITLE: Perilous Realms — All Game Commands
#+AUTHOR: generated from ~pr/src @ eb40d67
#+DATE: 2026-09-27
#+STARTUP: overview
#+OPTIONS: toc:2

* Conventions

Each command entry has properties drawn from the dispatch table
=cmd_info[]= in =h/inter.h=:

- =FILE= :: source file and line of the =do_*= handler
- =MIN_POS= :: minimum position the character must be in to use the command
  - =DEA= dead (usable in any state, incl. linkdead ghosts) · =MOR= mortally wounded
    · =INC= incapacitated · =STU= stunned · =SLE= sleeping · =RES= resting
    · =SIT= sitting · =PRY= praying · =FIG= fighting · =STA= standing
- =MIN_LEVEL= :: 0 = any mortal; a literal number = that player level;
  =I1=…=I11= = immortal ranks 2001–2011 (=h/const.h=)

Where a =do_*= function appears under several names in =cmd_info[]=, all
aliases are listed in the heading.

** Persistence vocabulary

All paths are relative to the game's lib directory (=~pr/live/lib=).

- =save_char(ch, room)= (=player.save.c=) :: writes the player file
  =stash/<first letter>/<name>=. Most commands do *not* call this directly;
  in-memory changes persist at the next routine save (tick, quit, rent).
- =save_acct= (=account.c=) :: writes =account/<c>/<name>=.
- =save_board= (=board.c=) :: writes =<vnum>.board=.
- =save_auc_data= (=auction.c=) :: writes =<name>-<room>.auction=.
- =save_world= / =full_save_world= (=world.save.c=) :: rewrite the world files (see the =saveworld= / =testsave= entries).
- =vlog= / =slog= :: append to the running game log (=~pr/live/logs/log=).

Where a command is purely informational it is marked *Read-only; no state change.*

* cmds1.c — movement, combat and group actions (19 commands)

** map (=do_map=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:649
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: map
: map auto
: map mini
: map overview [width [height]]
: map mortal                     (immortals only: force the mortal view)
: map short [width [height]]     (immortals only)
*** Parameters
- =mode=  :: =one_argument=; matched with =is_abbrev= against =mortal=, =overview=, =auto=, =short=, =mini=. An empty word matches nothing. Unknown words are silently ignored (default view).
- =width height=  :: only read after =short=/=overview=; two further =one_argument= tokens, =atoi= > 0 replaces the default 21x13; clamped to =MAX_GRID_X= (1024) x =MAX_GRID_Y= (256).
*** What it does
Draws an ASCII map of the rooms around the player by flood-filling a grid from =ch->in_room= (=grid_expand=), placing each neighbour one cell away in the compass direction of its exit; only exits that have a matching reverse exit are followed. Mortals (and immortals who type =mortal=) only expand into rooms flagged =MAPABLE=; if the current room is not =MAPABLE= the command silently switches to =auto= mode (9x5 in mapable rooms, else 7x5), which ignores the flag but does not pass closed exits. Immortals below =I9= standing in a non-mapable room outside their =build_lo..build_hi= and =test_lo..test_hi= ranges are likewise forced to the mortal auto view. Default sizes: mortal/=mini= 5x5, immortal 13x11 builder map (vnums and exit-count glyphs via =render_builder_map=), =auto= 13x5 for immortals, =short=/=overview= 21x13. Rendering is =render_standard_map= (sector-coloured glyphs, doors, diagonal exits; the viewer's room shows as a flashing =S=) except =overview= which uses =render_raw_rooms= (room glyphs only, no exits). Secret exits count toward the room glyph only for level >= =I1=; non-compass exits alter the glyph only above =I1=.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->in_room=, =ch->level=, =ch->build_lo/hi=, =ch->test_lo/hi=, room =exits= (=to_room=, =exit_info= EX_CLOSED/EX_HIDDEN/EX_ISDOOR, =num=), =room_flags= MAPABLE, =SectorWithRoom(rp)->map.*= glyph/colour tables, =IS_DARK=/=CAN_SEE_IN_DARK=.
- Writes :: file-static grid =rooms[][]= and the =point_q=/=point_end= work queue (reset on every call); no character or world state.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- Output only; nothing to persist. No log lines.

** priority (=do_priority=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:748
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: priority
*** Parameters
- none :: argument ignored.
*** What it does
Toggles the =PLR_PRIORITY= flag on the player's =act= bitvector and reports the new state ("Commands will [not] be ordered by priority."). The flag tells the interpreter to order the player's queued commands by the =priority= field of =cmd_info[]= instead of FIFO. NPCs are ignored (silent return).
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act= (=PLR_PRIORITY=), =IS_NPC(ch)=.
- Writes :: =ch->act ^= PLR_PRIORITY= (set or cleared).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- No direct save; in-memory only. =ch->act= is written by =save_char= (field 44), so the setting persists in =stash/<c>/<name>= at the next routine player save. No log lines.

** away (=do_away=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:762
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: away <message>
: away
*** Parameters
- =message= :: rest of the line after leading whitespace, stored verbatim as the away text.
*** What it does
Sets, replaces, or clears the player's away message. With text and not currently away: marks the player away ("You are now marked as away."). With text while already away: replaces the message ("Away message changed."). With no text while away: =clear_away= frees the message ("You are no longer marked as away."). With no text and not away: error "Please specify an away message." Being away is simply =ch->away != NULL= (=IS_AWAY=); other code (tells, who) reads it. NPCs are ignored.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->away= via =IS_AWAY(ch)=, =IS_NPC(ch)=.
- Writes :: =ch->away= (=Strdup= of the message, or =FREE=d to NULL).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- Session-only: =ch->away= is not among the fields written by =save_char=, so the away state is lost on quit/reboot. No log lines.

** puke (=do_puke=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:795
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: puke [target]        (social)
: puke all
*** Parameters
- =all=    :: =one_argument=; exact match (=str_cmp=) selects the real vomit action.
- anything else :: passed through to the social =do_action(ch, argument, -find_command("puke"))=.
*** What it does
"puke all" empties the character's stomach: room sees "$n pukes all over the place. Gross." If the player has not enabled hunger (=CONFIG_HUNGER= not set in =config_bits=) nothing else happens. Immortals get HUNGER/THIRST/DRUNK conditions and fullness set to -1 (never hungry). Mortals get HUNGER, THIRST and fullness set to 0 (DRUNK untouched) and then suffer one random penalty (=number(0,5)=): 0/1 = 3-tick =TYPE_PUKE_ALL= affect of -3 CON or -3 STR; 2 = lose 15 movement (=consume_moves=); 3 = lose 8 hp (floored at -1); 4 = 3-tick -1 =APPLY_HITNDAM=; 5 = nothing. "That didn't feel very good!" then =update_pos=. Any other argument behaves as the ordinary "puke" social.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->config_bits= (=CONFIG_HUNGER=), =IS_IMMORTAL(ch)=, =GET_HIT(ch)=.
- Writes :: =ch->conditions[HUNGER/THIRST/DRUNK]= (=SET_COND=), =ch->fullness= (=SET_FULL=), =ch->hit=, =ch->move=, =ch->affected= (via =affect_to_char=), position via =update_pos=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- No direct save; conditions (field 51), fullness (110), hit, move and the affect list are all written by =save_char= at the next routine save. No log lines.

** sing (=do_sing=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:856
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: sing                      (bard: flavour text; non-bard: social)
: sing <spell> [target]     (bard: casts, same syntax as cast)
*** Parameters
- =argument= :: rest of line. For bards, non-empty text is handed unchanged to =do_cast(ch, argument, cmd)= (=cmd= is =CMD_SING=, 68). For non-bards the whole line goes to the "sing" social.
*** What it does
Class-gated alias for casting. If =IsClass(ch, BARD)= is false the command is the plain "sing" social (=do_action= with =-find_command("sing")=). A bard with arguments casts through =do_cast= (so the spell parser, mana costs and cooldowns are exactly those of =cast=, with =cmd == CMD_SING= available to it). A bard with no argument just emits "You raise your melodious voice..." / "$n sings a battle hymn of old."
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: class via =IsClass(ch, BARD)=.
- Writes :: nothing directly; whatever =do_cast= or the social mutates.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none in this function. No log lines.

** slice (=do_slice=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:871
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: slice corpse
*** Parameters
- =corpse= :: the rest of the line must be exactly the word =corpse= (=str_cmp=); anything else prints "Syntax: slice corpse". The word is then used as the keyword for =get_obj_vis_accessible= (inventory or room).
*** What it does
Carves a piece of meat off a corpse. The corpse's =value[2]= (slices remaining, set in =make_corpse=) must be > 0, otherwise "There's not much left of that corpse..." and the room sees "$n picks through the remains of X." On success a brand-new prototype-less object (=CREATE= + =clear_object= + =setup_obj_ev=) is built: keywords "chunk slice <victim>", =ITEM_FOOD=, =ITEM_TAKE=, fills for 10 hours (=value[0]=10=), =MAT_ORGANIC=, one pound, =cost_per_day= 100. It is poisoned (=value[3]=1=) if =number(1,200) <= corpse->value[3]=. The corpse's =value[2]= is decremented and the slice is placed in the character's inventory (=obj_to_char=). The victim name is =corpse->name + 7=, i.e. the corpse keyword string with the leading "corpse " stripped.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =corpse->name=, =corpse->value[2]=, =corpse->value[3]=, =materials[MAT_ORGANIC].density=.
- Writes :: =corpse->value[2]--=; new =obj_data= (name, description, short_description, type_flag, wear_flags, value[0..3], cost_per_day, material, intrinsic_weight/volume) linked into =ch->carrying= via =obj_to_char= (carry weight/count updated).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- No direct save; the slice has =vnum= 0/no prototype, so it survives only as long as the inventory is saved with it (rent/quit path). No log lines.

** split (=do_split=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1011
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: split <coins>
*** Parameters
- =coins= :: rest of line after whitespace, =atoi= into =slong=. Must be 0 <= coins <= =GET_GOLD(ch)= ("But you only have N!" / "Get real."). Empty prints usage.
*** What it does
Divides gold evenly among group members in the room via =raw_split=. The group size (=num_in_group_and_room=) is 1 + the leader's followers who are =AFF_GROUP= and in the leader's room; fewer than 2 gives "No one in this room is in your group!", and a share of 0 gives "Try dividing up a little bit more!". Each qualifying follower (other than =ch=) and, if =ch= is not the leader, the leader (if grouped and in the room) receives =amount/ngroup=; every recipient is told who divided how much and their share. The splitter keeps the remainder (rounding). Room sees "$n divides up some gold."
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->gold=, =ch->master=, =leader->followers=, =IS_AFFECTED(x, AFF_GROUP)=, =in_room= of each member.
- Writes :: =ch->gold= (minus amount, plus remainder), =follower->gold += share=, =leader->gold += share=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- No direct save; =gold= (field 33) is written by =save_char= for each player at their next routine save. No log lines.

** scan (=do_scan=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1032
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: scan
*** Parameters
- none :: argument ignored.
*** What it does
Lists visible characters here and in adjacent rooms. Everyone in the current room that =CAN_SEE= and is not =ch= is listed as "Right here." Then for every exit that leads to a real room, is not =EX_CLOSED=, and is not a loop back to the same room, each visible occupant is listed with the exit's name with probability =percent = (60 + bonus) / 2= per person. =bonus= is +10 for DRUID/RANGER/FORESTER/CLASS_IMMORTAL, plus =race_track_bonus(ch)=, plus =get_skill(SKILL_SCAN)/10 + 1= if the skill is known (so an unskilled human sees ~30% of adjacent people). "You don't see anyone around." if nothing found. Room sees "$n furiously looks around." The 10-second =WAIT_STATE= is commented out. Flag =CMDF_NOARENA=: unusable in the arena.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =get_rp(ch->in_room)->people=, =->exits= (=to_room=, =exit_info=), =CAN_SEE=, class, race, =SKILL_SCAN=.
- Writes :: nothing.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No log lines.

** throw (=do_throw=) -- NOT REGISTERED
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1080
:MIN_POS: n/a (no cmd_info[] entry)
:MIN_LEVEL: n/a (no cmd_info[] entry)
:END:
*** Syntax
: throw <victim> [location]      (as coded; currently unreachable by players)
*** Parameters
- =victim=   :: first word (=argument_interpreter=), resolved with =get_char_room_vis=.
- =location= :: second word, only its first letter matters: =f=/=c=/=t= (feet/claws/tail) -> 1, =l= (legs) -> 2, =a=/=w=/=r= (arms/wings/trinket) -> 3, =h= (head) -> 5, anything else -> 4. Omitted -> =LOCATION_BODY=.
*** What it does
Despite the name and comment ("Throw an item in combat") this is an aimed melee attack: it sets =ch->aimloc= to the chosen body location and calls =hit(ch, victim, TYPE_UNDEFINED)=, then imposes =WAIT_STATE(PULSE_VIOLENCE + 2)=. Blocked by =check_peaceful= (PEACEFUL rooms, unless level > =I8= or in a PK world), by charm toward one's master, and unless the attacker is =POSITION_STANDING= and the victim is not the current =ch->fighting= target ("You do the best you can!"). Targeting yourself only prints "You hit yourself..OUCH!". The function has a prototype in the generated =h/fcns.h= but no line in =cmd_info[]= and no other caller, so it is dead code.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =GET_POS(ch)=, =ch->fighting=, =ch->master=, =IS_AFFECTED(ch, AFF_CHARM)=, room PEACEFUL flag.
- Writes :: =ch->aimloc=, =ch->wait= (=WAIT_STATE=); combat state via =hit()=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No log lines. (=aimloc= is not saved by =save_char=.)

** turn (=do_turn=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1156
:MIN_POS: SLE (sleeping)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: turn to page <number> in <book>
: turn <number> <book>          (any wording works: last word = book, first digit run = page)
*** Parameters
- =book=   :: =last_argument= -- the final word of the line; found with =generic_find= over room objects, equipment, inventory (and characters in room, which then fail the type check). Must be =ITEM_OTHER=.
- =number= :: =strpbrk(argument, "0123456789")= -- the first digit anywhere in the line, =atoi='d; a preceding =-= is honoured so negative page numbers are possible.
*** What it does
Opens a book to a given page. A "book" is an =ITEM_OTHER= object whose extra descriptions are keyed by page number; the command searches =obj->ex_description= for an entry whose keyword =atoi='s to the requested page. If found, =obj->value[0]= is set to that page ("You turn to page N in <book>") and the room sees "$n flips through $p"; otherwise "The book doesn't have a page N." Non-=ITEM_OTHER= targets: "You cannot turn to a page in the X." Only a NULL =argument= (never true from the interpreter) triggers the initial syntax message; an empty line reaches the "You do not see" / syntax branch at the end.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =obj->type_flag= (=GET_ITEM_TYPE=), =obj->ex_description= (=keyword=), =obj->short_description=.
- Writes :: =obj->value[0] = page= (current page of the book).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- No direct save; the page is an object value, saved with the object wherever it lives (player inventory/equipment at the next player save; room objects are not saved). No log lines.

** aggr (=do_aggr=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1220
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: aggr
: aggr off
: aggr <hp>
*** Parameters
- none  :: reports current setting.
- =off= :: exact =strcmp=; disables.
- =hp=  :: =one_argument=, =atoi=, stored (cast to short) as the hit-point floor; any non-numeric word becomes 0.
*** What it does
Player-side auto-aggression toward aggressive monsters (per the header comment: attack =ACT_AGGRESSIVE= mobs immediately on entering a room). =ch->aggressive == -1= means off ("You are not aggressive to monsters"); otherwise the value is the hp threshold below which the player stops auto-attacking ("You will be aggressive unless hp < N"). NPCs are ignored. The actual attack logic lives elsewhere (movement/mobact); this command only sets the field.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->aggressive=, =IS_NPC(ch)=.
- Writes :: =ch->aggressive= (-1 or the threshold).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- Session-only: =ch->aggressive= is not written by =save_char=, so the setting is lost on quit/reboot. No log lines.

** gsay, gtalk (=do_gsay=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1261
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: gsay <message>
: gtalk <message>
*** Parameters
- =message= :: rest of line after leading whitespace; empty -> "Yes, but WHAT do you want to gsay?"
*** What it does
Group channel. Refused for players flagged =PLR_STUPID= or on an account flagged =A_STUPID= ("Stop being stupid or go away."), and for anyone not =AFF_GROUP= ("But you are a member of no group?!"). Determines the leader (=ch->master= or =ch=), then delivers "$n group-says '<msg>'" via =act(..., TO_VICT)= to the leader (if the speaker is not the leader) and to every follower of the leader who is =AFF_GROUP=, regardless of room -- it works across the world like a conference channel. Speaker sees "Ok." unless =PLR_ECHO= is set, in which case "You group-say '<msg>'" (=fix_spec= escapes the text).
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act= (=PLR_STUPID=, =PLR_ECHO=), account flags, =IS_AFFECTED(AFF_GROUP)=, =ch->master=, =master->followers=.
- Writes :: nothing.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No log lines.

** consent (=do_consent=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1307
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: consent <player>
: consent
*** Parameters
- =player= :: =one_argument=, resolved with =get_char_vis= (anywhere visible, not just the room); must be a PC (=IS_NPC= target -> "No one by that name here ...."). Flag =CMDF_NOORDER=: cannot be forced via =order=.
*** What it does
Grants one other player permission to handle your corpse/belongings (checked by =corpse_consent= and friends). With no argument, revokes any existing consent: the previous consentee, if online (=get_char=), is told "You no longer have X's consent!". With a target: if consent already exists it is revoked first (with the same notification); if the target is the same person as the existing consentee the command acts as a toggle and stops there. Otherwise =ch->consent= becomes the target's name, both parties are notified. Only one consentee at a time. NPCs cannot use it.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->consent=, =target->name=, =IS_NPC=.
- Writes :: =ch->consent= (=Strdup(target->name)= or =FREE=d).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- Session-only: =ch->consent= is not written by =save_char=; consent is lost on quit/reboot.
- Logs (=vlog(LOG_CONSENT, ...)=, visible to immortals >= =I7= per =log.c=): "Consent: remove <old> from <ch>" on every revoke, "Consent: grant <new> from <ch>" on every grant.

** report (=do_report=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1359
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: report
: report <extra text>
*** Parameters
- =extra text= :: rest of line after leading spaces; appended after the stats as ". <text>".
*** What it does
Announces the speaker's vitals to the room as "$n reports '<hp>/<max>H <mana>/<max>M <power>/<max>P <move>/<max>V'", each current value colour-coded by percentage (=COLOR_CODE(PERCENT_xx)=). If =GET_SHIELD(ch) > 0= the report is prefixed with "({<shield>})". Blocked for =PLR_STUPID= / =A_STUPID=. Speaker sees "Ok."
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =GET_HIT/GET_MAX_HIT=, =GET_MANA/GET_MAX_MANA=, =GET_POWER/GET_MAX_POWER=, =GET_MOVE/GET_MAX_MOVE=, =ch->hit_shield= (=GET_SHIELD=), =ch->act= (=PLR_STUPID=), account flags.
- Writes :: nothing.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No log lines.

** hitall (=do_hitall=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1399
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: hitall
: hitall all
*** Parameters
- none  :: attack only NPCs flagged =ACT_AGGRESSIVE=.
- =all=  :: exact =strcmp= after =one_argument=; attack every visible NPC in the room.
*** What it does
Warrior sweep attack. Refused by =check_peaceful= ("You feel ashamed to try to disrrupt the tranquility..."). If =get_skill(SKILL_HITALL) <= 0= the room sees "$n makes a fool of himself..." and the player "Maybe when you learn it!" -- but the code does NOT return, so the loop still runs (with skill 0 no hit ever lands, but moves are still spent). For every character in the room the player can see (PCs included), 1 movement point is consumed (=consume_moves=); then for each NPC that qualifies (aggressive, or any NPC with =all=) a roll =number(1,131)= is made and =hit(ch, mob, TYPE_UNDEFINED)= fires if the skill percent is >= the roll; qualifying NPCs are counted whether or not the blow lands. PCs are never attacked. Finally =WAIT_STATE(ch, WAIT_SEC * count)= (one second of lag per target).
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =get_rp(ch->in_room)->people=, =CAN_SEE=, =mob->act= (=ACT_AGGRESSIVE=), =SKILL_HITALL=, room PEACEFUL flag.
- Writes :: =ch->move= (via =consume_moves=, floored at -25% of max), =ch->wait=; combat state via =hit()=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. =move= is saved by the next routine =save_char=. No log lines.

** aid, bandage (=do_aid=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1454
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: aid
: aid <victim>
: bandage [victim]
*** Parameters
- =victim= :: =one_argument=; =get_char_room_vis= ("You don't see that person here."). Omitted -> self.
*** What it does
First aid for someone who is bleeding out. Only works if the target's =GET_HIT <= 0= (mortally wounded/incapacitated/stunned range) and is not already under =SKILL_AID=; otherwise "$N's wounds aren't bleeding." / "Your wounds aren't bleeding." Heals =number(1,8)= hp capped at max, runs =update_pos= (so the target may wake to a better position), and applies a 10-tick =SKILL_AID= marker affect (=APPLY_NONE=) that prevents another aid. Messages to actor, target and room. There is no skill check -- anyone standing can do it. The "to no avail" branches are dead because =heal= is never 0.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =GET_HIT(vict)=, =GET_MAX_HIT(vict)=, =affected_by_spell(vict, SKILL_AID)=.
- Writes :: =vict->hit=, =vict->affected= (SKILL_AID affect via =affect_to_char=), position via =update_pos=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. =hit= and the affect list are saved for the victim at their next routine =save_char=. No log lines.

** appraise (=do_appraise=) -- NOT REGISTERED
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:1538
:MIN_POS: n/a (no cmd_info[] entry)
:MIN_LEVEL: n/a (no cmd_info[] entry)
:END:
*** Syntax
: appraise <item>       (as coded; currently unreachable by players)
*** Parameters
- =item= :: =one_argument=; must be in inventory (=get_obj_in_list_vis(ch, name, ch->carrying)=), "You can't appraise an item that you don't have."
*** What it does
Skill-based partial identify. A deterministic per-player/per-item bonus in 0..44 is derived by hashing =ch->name= + first keyword of =object->name= (=ComputeHashValueForKey % 45=), added to =get_skill(SKILL_APPRAISE)=; immortals get 200. That percent gates how much =identify_new= reveals (flags at 50, special-proc hint at 60, scroll/potion spells at 70, weapon damage at 75, wand/staff at 80, value/armor at 85, applies at 110). Room sees "$n looks quizically at the $o." =PLR_DEBUG= players also see the skill and bonus. The function has no =cmd_info[]= entry and no other caller in the tree (only a generated prototype in =h/fcns.h=), so it is dead code; the value-estimate part of =examine= is what players actually get.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, =object->name=, =ch->name=, =SKILL_APPRAISE=, =ch->act= (=PLR_DEBUG=), and everything =identify_new= reads.
- Writes :: nothing.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No log lines.

** examine, describe (=do_examine=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:2093
:MIN_POS: RES (resting) for examine; DEA (dead) for describe
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: examine <item>
: examine <item set name>
: describe <item>              (same output, also spoken to the room)
*** Parameters
- =item= :: =one_argument=; =generic_find= over inventory, room objects and equipment. If no object matches it is tried as an item-set name (=get_item_set_by_name_char=). =cmd == CMD_DESCRIBE= (87) sets =say=, making every =send_or_sayf= line also =act= to the room.
*** What it does
Detailed look at an object. If the word names an item set the player either has a set bonus from or carries a piece of, prints the set header (vnum shown to immortals), description, set cooldowns, all ten set-bonus tiers (=get_setinfo_bonuses=) and the list of items in the set, then returns. If nothing matched, =examine= falls back to =do_look(ch, argument, cmd)= (so "examine <mob>" or "examine <extra desc>" behaves like look) while =describe= says "You don't see anything like that." For an object: material, weight (decim), volume (dolum), dimensions if any; a value estimate =cost * (hash+75) / 100= when =50 + hash < get_skill(SKILL_APPRAISE)= where =hash = hash_string(fname(obj->name) + ch->name) % 50= (deterministic per player/item); socket count; socket-gem facts for =ITEM_SOCKET= items; "Part of set" for non-common set pieces; socket bonuses (mortals only -- immortals see them inside =identify_new=). Containers stop here for mortals. Finally, common-rarity items (any level) and every item for immortals get the full =identify_new(ch, obj, 200, say)= dump; uncommon+ items stay opaque to mortals. In =describe= mode the room first sees "$n examines $p and describes it to the room:".
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, =ch->equipment=, room contents, =obj->material=, weight/volume, =length/width/height=, =cost=, =sockets=, =value[]=, =item_set=, =rarity=, =socket_apply[]=, =SKILL_APPRAISE=, =IS_IMMORTAL=, item-set tables.
- Writes :: nothing (=identify_new= may call =get_and_set_gem_removal_costs= for socket gems, which can cache costs on the object).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No log lines.

** berserk (=do_berserk=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds1.c:2334
:MIN_POS: FIG (fighting; i.e. fighting or standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: berserk
*** Parameters
- none :: argument ignored; the rage type is random ("No option allowed").
*** What it does
Barbarian rage. Refused if already under the =SKILL_BERSERK= pulse affect, if =AFF_BERRECV= (recovering) is set, or if =get_skill(SKILL_BERSERK) < 1=. Success chance: =skill + WIS= (+ half the =REMORT_IMPROVED_BERSERK= stat bonus, x =REMORT_ADVANCED_SKILL_BERSERK= multiplier) versus =number(1,141)=; immortals always succeed. On failure the player still receives the recovery penalty (=berserk_setRecover= with a short delay). On success a random type 0-5 is applied by =berserk_setBerserk=: a stacking pulse buff (=stack_multiplier= 1.2 x remort bonus, proc every =proc_frequency= pulses for =how_long=) on MELEE_PENETRATION, HITNDAM, DAMROLL, MELEE_HASTE, HITROLL or LIFE_STEAL, with an immediate +DAMROLL bonus (=modifier_initial=, min 5). =berserk_setBerserk= first calls =check_spell_skill_cooldown(SKILL_BERSERK)=; if the skill is on cooldown it prints the cooldown message and applies nothing -- but =do_berserk= has already printed the success text and still applies the recovery. Non-immortals then get =berserk_setRecover(how_long+1)=: an ordinary affect =SKILL_BERSERK_RECOVER= (negative =APPLY_ALL_STOPPING=, sets =AFF_BERRECV=, up to 5 ticks) plus a delayed negative =APPLY_MELEE_HASTE= pulse debuff that begins when the rage ends. Durations, frequencies and modifiers all scale with level, =remort_count= and the two remort effects.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =SKILL_BERSERK=, =GET_WIS/STR/DEX/INT/CON=, =GET_LEVEL=, =ch->remort_count=, remort effects/stat bonuses, =ch->affected_pulse=, =ch->affected_pulse_cooldown=, =IS_AFFECTED(AFF_BERRECV)=.
- Writes :: =ch->affected_pulse= (SKILL_BERSERK buff, SKILL_BERSERK_RECOVER debuff via =pulse_affect_to_char=), =ch->affected= (SKILL_BERSERK_RECOVER affect with =AFF_BERRECV= via =affect_to_char=), and any cooldown entry =pulse_affect_to_char= creates.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- No direct save; the affect, pulse-affect and pulse-cooldown lists are all written by =save_char= at the next routine save, so an in-progress rage/recovery survives a quit. No log lines.

** Helpers in cmds1.c
- =visitedPoint= (35) :: linear scan of the =rooms[][]= grid for a vnum; used by =grid_expand= to avoid placing a room twice.
- =queue_point= (47) :: appends an (x, y, vnum) node to the =point_q= breadth-first work list (=CREATE=d, freed in =do_map=).
- =grid_expand= (59) :: for one grid cell, walks the room's exits and places each unvisited neighbour in the adjacent cell for its compass direction; honours =MAPABLE= (mortal mode), skips closed exits (auto mode), and requires a reverse exit (=find_reverse_exit=).
- =render_map_str_for_room= (117) :: appends the coloured one-character glyph for a room to a buffer: sector glyph set (default " ^v:"), up/down/other-exit variants (secret exits hidden below =I1=, exit counts only above =I1=), dark-room colour, and a flashing =S= for the viewer's room. Also used by =render_raw_rooms=.
- =render_exit_for_direction= (166) :: appends the wall/exit/door/open-door glyph and colour for one direction of a room, from the sector's =map.walls/exits/doors/odoors= tables; hidden exits render as wall.
- =render_fill_for_sector= / =render_fill_for_room= (216/223) :: append a sector-coloured blank cell.
- =render_corner_exit= (229) :: resolves what to draw at the SE corner between four cells (diagonal exits, crossing diagonals -> =CROSS=).
- =render_room_with_exits= (271) :: renders one grid cell into the two output lines (room+east exit, south exit+corner).
- =render_standard_map= (301) :: builds the two-lines-per-row standard map and hands it to =render_final_map=.
- =render_builder_map= (338) :: immortal map: 6-digit vnums per cell, colour/glyph by exit type, current room bold/underlined.
- =render_final_map= (517) :: trims common leading whitespace and blank rows, then =sendf=s the map lines (prefixed =\6= to bypass formatting).
- =render_raw_rooms= (543) :: overview renderer: one glyph per cell, no exits.
- =clear_away= (789) :: frees =ch->away= and prints "You are no longer marked as away."; also called from outside this file.
- =Nice_Name= (940) :: capitalised short name of =ch= as seen by =seer=, in a static buffer.
- =in_group_and_room= (949) :: true if two chars are grouped (=in_group=), both =AFF_GROUP=, and in the same room.
- =num_in_group_and_room= (955) :: 1 + number of the leader's followers that are grouped and in the leader's room.
- =raw_split= (970) :: does the gold arithmetic and messaging for =split=; subtracts =amount= from =ch->gold=, adds a share to each grouped member in the room, returns the remainder to =ch=.
- =ComputeHashValueForKey= (1527) :: 15-bit polynomial string hash used by =do_appraise= for the deterministic bonus.
- =calc_average_weapon_damage= (1583) :: =value[1] * (1 + value[2]) / 2= plus any DAMROLL/HITNDAM applies.
- =calc_weapon_rating= (1596) :: average damage divided by pi -- the "rating" shown by identify.
- =calc_damage_reduction_rating= (1601) :: armour "rating": =3*value[3]= plus weighted stopping-power applies, plus material toughness and a metal bonus.
- =identify_new= (1624) :: the shared identify dump used by =appraise=, =examine= and the identify spell; =percent= gates each section (50 flags, 60 special proc, 70 scroll/potion spells, 75 weapon damage, 80 wand/staff, 85 value/armour, 110 applies, cooldowns, item abilities); prints set info, socket bonuses, and recursively identifies rare items inside a container the viewer has consent for. =say= mirrors every line to the room.
- =berserk_setRecover= (2251) :: applies the post-rage weakness: =SKILL_BERSERK_RECOVER= affect (negative ALL_STOPPING, sets =AFF_BERRECV=) plus a delayed stacking MELEE_HASTE pulse debuff.
- =berserk_setBerserk= (2279) :: applies the rage pulse buff of the chosen type after checking the skill cooldown.

* cmds2.c — communication and writing (22 commands)

** say, ' (=do_say=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:16
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: say <text>
: ' <text>
*** Parameters
- =text= :: rest of line, leading whitespace skipped. Empty -> "Yes, but WHAT do you want to say?"
*** What it does
Room-local speech. Refused if the player or their account is flagged stupid (=PLR_STUPID= / =A_STUPID=). Calls =unsneak= (drops sneak/prowl/vanish/shimmer), then sends "$n says '<text>'" (colour code 4) to everyone awake in the room via =act(...,FALSE_IGNORE,...,TO_ROOM)=, which honours the listeners' ignore lists and does not hide invisible speakers. If the speaker has =CONFIG_TIMESTAMPS= the message is prefixed with "(HH:MM)". The speaker sees "You say '...'" if NPC or =PLR_ECHO= is set, otherwise just "Ok.". Speaking clears an away message.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act= (=PLR_STUPID=, =PLR_ECHO=), =ch->desc->acct->flags=, =ch->config_bits=, =ch->away=, room people list
- Writes :: affects removed by =unsneak=; =ch->away= freed by =clear_away= if set
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save.

** shout (=do_shout=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:102
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: shout <text>
: shout -l <text>          (immortals: local shout, propagate 5 rooms instead of world-wide)
*** Parameters
- =-l= :: literal flag "-l " at start of argument; sets =local=. Only changes behaviour for immortals.
- =text= :: rest of line, leading spaces skipped. Empty -> "Shout? Yes! Fine! Shout we must, but WHAT??"
*** What it does
Refused for =PLR_STUPID=/=A_STUPID=, for players with =PLR_NOSHOUT= or account =A_NOSHOUT= ("You can't shout!"), and for charmed followers (=AFF_CHARM= with a master; the master is told "I don't think so :-)"). Calls =unsneak=, echoes to the speaker (NPC or =PLR_ECHO=) or "Ok.", and sends "$n shouts '...'" (colour 9) to the room. For mortals and mobs (or immortals using =-l=) the message is then flood-propagated through exits up to 5 rooms away by =propagate_message=; mobs are named, players appear as "Someone shouts". Rooms already reached are marked =visited=. Mobs stop here. For players, a final pass over =connection_list= sends the shout to every playing character not in the shouter's room whose room was not already reached and who is not =PLR_DEAF=, but only if that listener is a switched immortal (=d->original=), an immortal, =PLR_POLICE=, or the shouter is an immortal not using =-l= (i.e. immortal shouts are world-wide). Listeners who =CAN_SEE= the shouter get the name, others "Someone". Clears the away message. Note: for immortal non-local shouts =propagate_message= is skipped, so the =rp->visited= test uses stale flags from the previous propagation.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act= (=PLR_STUPID=, =PLR_NOSHOUT=, =PLR_ECHO=, =PLR_DEAF=), account flags, =ch->master=, =ch->affected_by=, =ch->in_room=, =connection_list=, =d->original=, =room_data.visited=, =room_data.exits=
- Writes :: every =room_data.visited=/=parent= reset to 0 by =hash_iterate(clear_visited)=, then set to 1 for rooms reached; =ch->away= freed if set; affects removed by =unsneak=
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- No direct save; in-memory only.

** chat, / (=do_chat=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:176
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009) (or granted via =is_granted=)
:END:
*** Syntax
: chat <text>
: / <text>
*** Parameters
- =text= :: rest of line, not trimmed. Empty -> "What do you want to chat about?"
*** What it does
High-immortal chat channel. Silently ignored for NPCs. Sends "\5(HH:MM) <name>> <text>" (byte 0x05 is the channel-colour marker; timestamp always included) to every playing connection whose real character (=i->original= if switched, else =i->character=) is level I9..=ABS_MAX_LVL= (2012) or has been explicitly granted this command (=is_granted(v,-cmd)=; =cmd= arrives as the negated =cmd_info= index because the table entry has =num= 0). If the *sender* has =CONFIG_CHANNAMES= the line is prefixed "[iCHAT] " for all recipients. No ignore, deaf or silence checks; sender receives their own copy through the loop.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =connection_list=, =i->connected=, =i->original=, =GET_LEVEL=, =v->granted=, account =granted= list, =ch->config_bits=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- No state change; nothing to save.

** imp (=do_impchat=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:203
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I10 (2010)
:END:
*** Syntax
: imp <text>
*** Parameters
- =text= :: rest of line, not trimmed. Empty -> "What do you want to imp chat about?"
*** What it does
Implementor-only channel. Even if reached via a grant, a caller below I10 is refused ("Impchat is only for the most powerful imms.") and the attempt is logged. NPCs are silently ignored. Sends "[IMP] <name>> <text>" in white to every playing connection whose real character (original if switched) is level I10..=ABS_MAX_LVL=. No timestamp (code commented out), no grant path, no ignore checks.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->level=, =connection_list=, =i->original=, =i->connected=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- Log: =vlog(LOG_MISC, "%s (Level %d) tried to use impchat, but isn't powerful enough.")= on refusal.

** wcalc (=do_wcalc=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:233
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I8 (2008)
:END:
*** Syntax
: wcalc <dice1> <dice2> <damroll>
*** Parameters
- =dice1= :: number of dice (=atoi=), via =three_argument_interpreter=
- =dice2= :: die size (=atoi=)
- =damroll= :: flat damage bonus (=atoi=). If the third argument is missing -> usage message.
*** What it does
Builder utility. Computes integer average damage =dice1*(1+dice2)/2 + damroll= and a "weapon rating" of =avdam/3.14159=, and prints both. Pure calculation; no game state touched. Note the function is declared with two parameters =(ch, arg)= although the dispatcher passes three; harmless with the untyped =void (*)()= pointer.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: nothing beyond the arguments
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- No state change.

** wiznet, ; (=do_commune=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:272
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I1 (2001) (or granted)
:END:
*** Syntax
: wiznet <text>
: ; <text>
*** Parameters
- =text= :: rest of line, not trimmed (no empty-argument check; an empty wiznet is broadcast).
*** What it does
Immortal chat channel. NPCs are ignored when invoked as a command. Builds "\5(HH:MM) <name>: <text>" (prefixed "[WizNet] " if the *sender* has =CONFIG_CHANNAMES=) and sends it to every playing connection whose real character (original if switched) is level I1..=ABS_MAX_LVL= and has not had this command revoked (=is_revoked(v,-cmd)=), or who has been granted it (=is_granted(v,-cmd)=). =cmd= is the negated =cmd_info= index, so grant/revoke lists are checked against the actual command index. A =cmd == -1= path broadcasts =argument= verbatim (intended for internal callers; none exist in the tree). No ignore/deaf checks; sender hears their own line.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =connection_list=, =i->connected=, =i->original=, =GET_LEVEL=, =granted=/=revoked= lists on char and account, =ch->config_bits=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- No state change.

** tell, reply, =, tellmore, - (=do_tell=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:299
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: tell <name> <text>
: reply <text>               (= is an alias; to the last person who told you)
: reply                      (no text: sets your tellmore target to them)
: tellmore <text>            (- is an alias; to the last person you told)
*** Parameters
- =name= :: first word via =half_chop= (tell form only); resolved with =get_char_vis= (any visible char in the world, PC or NPC)
- =text= :: rest of line. For =reply=/=tellmore= the whole argument (leading whitespace skipped). Dispatch is by =cmd=: =CMD_TELL= (71), =CMD_REPLY= (74), =CMD_TELLMORE= (75).
*** What it does
Private world-wide message. Refused for =PLR_STUPID=/=A_STUPID=. =reply= needs =ch->last_tell_from=, =tellmore= needs =ch->last_tell_to=; with no text, =reply= just copies the last sender into =last_tell_to= and returns. Target must be visible and not yourself; the target name is recorded in =ch->last_tell_to=. Blocks, in order: target asleep (unless sender >= I1); target is an NPC without a descriptor -> hands "<sender> <text>" to the mob's spec proc with =SPEC_CMD=/=CMD_TELL= and otherwise says "No-one by that name here"; target link-dead; target =PLR_VEG= (writing) unless sender >= I10; target =PLR_NOTELL= unless sender >= I5; sender =PLR_NOTELL= unless >= I10 (the =is_granted(ch,-cmd)= escape can never match because =cmd= is a positive =CMD_= number here, so lower-level notell senders are always refused); target =PLR_QUESTING= while sender is not, unless sender >= I5; target's account ignores sender; sender ignores target unless >= I5. If the target is away, their away message is shown. The message "#{b}<name or Someone> tells you '...'" (timestamp if the *receiver* has =CONFIG_TIMESTAMPS=) is sent; a vegged target gets it queued directly with =write_to_q= so it appears after their edit. Sender sees "You tell X" (NPC or =PLR_ECHO=) or "Ok.". If the target can see the sender, =vict->last_tell_from= is set to the sender's name. Clears sender's away. Warns "They are VEGGED... sent anyways." Senders >= I10 are then told about any snoopers (below I11) on either party.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act=, =vict->act= (=PLR_VEG=, =PLR_NOTELL=, =PLR_QUESTING=, =PLR_ECHO=), account flags, =ch->last_tell_from=, =ch->last_tell_to=, =vict->desc=, =vict->desc->snooped_by[]=, =ch->desc->snooped_by[]=, =GET_POS(vict)=, =vict->away=, =ch->config_bits=, =vict->config_bits=, =real_mobp(vict->vnum)->func=, account ignore lists
- Writes :: =ch->last_tell_to= (freed/strdup'd), =vict->last_tell_from= (freed/Strdup'd), =ch->away= freed if set; =vict->desc->output= queue when target is vegged
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- =last_tell_from=/=last_tell_to= are not written by =save_char= at all; they live only for the session. Other state in-memory only.

** whisper (=do_whisper=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:467
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 2
:END:
*** Syntax
: whisper <name> <text>
*** Parameters
- =name= :: first word via =half_chop=, resolved with =get_char_room_vis= (same room only)
- =text= :: rest of line. Either missing -> "Who do you want to whisper to.. and what??"
*** What it does
Room-private message. Refused for =PLR_STUPID=/=A_STUPID=. Whispering to yourself emotes "$n whispers quietly to $mself." Refuses sleeping targets and targets whose account is ignoring you (checked twice, redundantly). Otherwise the target gets "$n whispers to you, '<text>'", the whisperer sees "You whisper to X" (NPC or =PLR_ECHO=) or "Ok.", and the rest of the room sees "$n whispers something to $N." Clears away. Does not call =unsneak=.

:PROPERTIES:

:END:
*** Syntax
: ask <name> <text>
*** Parameters
- =name= :: first word via =half_chop=, =get_char_room_vis= (same room)
- =text= :: rest of line
*** What it does
Same as whisper but phrased as a question: target gets "$n asks you '<text>'", room sees "$n asks $N a question.", self-target emotes "$n quietly asks $mself a question." Refused for stupid flags and when the target's account ignores you. Unlike whisper it does not refuse sleeping targets (though =act= only delivers to awake recipients, so a sleeping target simply never sees it). Clears away.

** write (=do_write=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:551
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal); flags =CMDF_NOORDER= (cannot be forced via order)
:END:
*** Syntax
: write <paper> [<pen>]
*** Parameters
- =paper= :: first word (=argument_interpreter=), object in inventory (=get_obj_in_list_vis=), must be =ITEM_NOTE=
- =pen= :: optional second word; must be =ITEM_PEN= in inventory. If omitted, any =ITEM_PEN= in inventory suffices (=find_obj_of_type_in_inventory=).
*** What it does
Starts the line editor on a blank note. Refused for stupid flags, or if the character has no descriptor, or if the note already has text ("There's something written on it already."). Tells the room "$n begins to jot down a note." and points the connection's string editor at =paper->action_description= with a 4000-byte limit (=MAX_NOTE_LENGTH=). Subsequent input lines are appended by =string_add= (modify.c) until a line ending in "@"; overlong text is truncated. Note: this does not set =PLR_VEG=; that is done elsewhere.

- Writes :: =ch->desc->str = &paper->action_description=, =ch->desc->max_str = 4000=; the note text itself is later written by =string_add=
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- Text lives on the object; persisted with the player's inventory by the next routine save.

** notereply (=do_reply=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:609
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: notereply <paper> [<pen>]
*** Parameters
- =paper= :: first word, inventory object; must already have =action_description= (type is NOT checked, any object with text works)
- =pen= :: optional; same rules as =write=
*** What it does
Reply-in-place on an existing note (the player-typed command is =notereply=; =reply= goes to =do_tell=). Refused for stupid flags, missing descriptor, or a note with nothing written ("There's nothing written on it already to reply to."). Stashes the current text in =ch->desc->reply_str=, clears the note's text, and opens the editor on it (4000 bytes). When the editor terminates, =string_add= calls =create_reply= to append the old text quoted with "> " after the newly written reply (on failure the original text is restored). Room sees "$n begins to jot down a note."
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act=, account flags, =ch->carrying=, =pen->type_flag=, =paper->action_description=
- Writes :: =ch->desc->reply_str= (takes ownership of old text), =paper->action_description = NULL=, =ch->desc->str=, =ch->desc->max_str = 4000=
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- Text lives on the object; persisted with inventory on the next routine save.

** quote (=do_quote=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:665
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: quote <from-paper> <to-paper>
*** Parameters
- =from-paper= :: first word, inventory =ITEM_NOTE= with text
- =to-paper= :: second word, a different inventory =ITEM_NOTE= that already has your reply written on it
*** What it does
Appends the source note's text, quoted line-by-line with "> ", onto the end of the destination note via =create_reply=. Both must be notes, distinct, and both must already have text ("You must write your reply first." if the destination is blank). No stupid-flag check, no pen needed, no editor session. Bug: the "You have no %s." messages pass the NULL object pointer instead of the typed name, so they print "(null)" or misbehave.

- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, =GET_ITEM_TYPE= of both, both =action_description=
- Writes :: =dest->action_description= replaced (freed and strdup'd) by =create_reply=
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- Log: =create_reply= logs =vlog(LOG_URGENT, "%s is trying to crash the mud with a note.")= if the combined text would exceed =MAX_STRING_LENGTH=.
- Object text persisted with inventory on the next routine save.

** mail (=do_mail=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:718
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: mail <note> <character>
*** Parameters
- =note= :: first word (=one_argument=), inventory =ITEM_NOTE= with text
- =character= :: second word; a player name. Matched first against online PCs by =isname(to, v->namelist)=; otherwise treated as an offline player file name.
*** What it does
Mobs just emote "tries in vain to summon a mail daemon." Refused for stupid flags, missing note, non-note, blank note, or a note whose text +255 would exceed =MAX_STRING_LENGTH= (logged as a crash attempt). Builds "From: <sender>\nTo: <Name>\nDate: <asctime minus year>\n\n<text>". If the recipient is online (any non-NPC in =character_list=): refused if their account ignores you; otherwise the note object is moved from sender to recipient with its text replaced by the headed version, the room and recipient see the mail-daemon messages, =new_notes= is bumped if the recipient is link-dead, and the recipient is saved immediately. If not online: =SendMail= (player.save.c:56) opens =stash/<c>/<name>= read/write, seeks back over the trailing =EOF_MARK= and appends a =MAIL= string record plus a new =EOF_MARK=; the note is then destroyed (=extract_obj=). At the recipient's next login (nanny.c:851) each =MAIL= record becomes a fresh =NOTE_NUMBER= (vnum 1000) object in their inventory. If no such player file exists: "<name> is unknown to me." and the note is kept. No ignore check on the offline path.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act=, account flags, =ch->carrying=, =paper->type_flag=, =paper->action_description=, =character_list=, =v->namelist=, account ignore lists, =v->desc=
- Writes :: online: =paper= moved (=obj_from_char=/=obj_to_char=), =paper->action_description= replaced, =v->new_notes++= if link-dead. Offline: =paper= extracted.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: online recipient: =save_char(v, NOWHERE)= -> =stash/<c>/<name>=. Offline recipient: =SendMail= appends a =MAIL= record directly to =stash/<c>/<name>=.
- Sender is not saved here (note removal persists on their next routine save).
- Log: =vlog(LOG_URGENT, "%s is trying to crash the mud with a note.")= on oversize note; =SendMail= logs =LOG_DEBUG "Seek failed."= on fseek error.

*** What it does
Toggles the caller's subscription to a public channel. NPCs ignored. For "sports" the bit is a NO-flag, so setting it tunes out; for auction/gossip setting the bit tunes in. Prints "You have now tuned into/out of the X channel." When a mortal tunes into gossip, =do_help(ch, "gossip rules", 0)= is shown.

** auction (=do_auction=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:872
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 1
:END:
*** Syntax
: auction <text>
*** Parameters
- =text= :: rest of line, leading spaces skipped. Empty -> "Auction? Yes! Fine! Auction we must, but WHAT??"
*** What it does
World-wide auction channel (chat only; unrelated to auction.c's item auctions). Refused for =PLR_STUPID=/=A_STUPID=, for =PLR_SILENCE=/=A_SILENCE= ("You can't auction!"), and for players not tuned in (=PLR_AUCTION= unset; mobs skip this). Calls =unsneak=. Echo to sender if =PLR_ECHO= (players only), else "Ok.". Sends "<name or Someone> auctions '<text>'" wrapped in MXP <Auction> tags to every playing, non-mob character other than the sender who has =PLR_AUCTION= set and whose account is not ignoring the sender; timestamp shown if the *receiver* has =CONFIG_TIMESTAMPS=. Text is MXP-escaped per receiver. Clears away.

** gossip (=do_gossip=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds2.c:952
:MIN_POS: SLE (sleeping)
:MIN_LEVEL: 1
:END:
*** Syntax
: gossip <text>
*** Parameters
- =text= :: rest of line, leading spaces skipped. Empty -> "Gossip? Yes! Fine! Gossip we must, but WHAT??"
*** What it does
World-wide gossip channel. Charmed/pet NPCs (=IS_NPC= with a master) are silently ignored. Players are refused for =PLR_STUPID=/=A_STUPID=, =PLR_SILENCE=/=A_SILENCE= ("You can't gossip!"), or when not tuned in (=PLR_GOSSIP= unset). Calls =unsneak=. Echo to sender if =PLR_ECHO= (players only), else "Ok.". Sends cyan "<name or Someone> gossips '<text>'" in MXP <Gossip> tags to every playing character other than the sender; player recipients must have =PLR_GOSSIP= set, mob recipients (switched imms) always receive; recipients whose account ignores the sender are skipped. Timestamp per receiver's =CONFIG_TIMESTAMPS=. Clears away.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->act=, =ch->master=, account flags, =connection_list=, =v->act=, =v->config_bits=, account ignore lists
- Writes :: =ch->away= freed if set; affects removed by =unsneak=
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none
- No direct save; in-memory only.

- =text= :: rest of line, leading spaces skipped. Empty -> "Yes.. But what?"
*** What it does
Free-form room action: everyone awake in the room sees "<bold>$n <text>" via =act(...,FALSE_IGNORE,...,TO_ROOM)= (honours ignore lists). Refused for =PLR_STUPID=/=A_STUPID=. NPCs without a descriptor may only emote when called internally with =cmd == 0= (e.g. from =do_mail=); via the dispatcher =cmd= is non-zero so mob spec-procs cannot emote this way. Calls =unsneak=, replies "Ok." to the actor, clears away.

The old text is gone as soon as the command is entered, even if the editor is abandoned.

** Helpers in cmds2.c
- =clear_visited= (54) :: =hash_iterate= callback; zeroes =rp->visited= and =rp->parent= on one room. Used to reset the BFS scratch flags before a shout propagation.
- =prop_mess= (60, static) :: recursive flood: if the room is unvisited, sends =m= to every connected occupant who is a mob or not =PLR_DEAF=; marks the room visited and recurses through each exit with =dist-1=. Stops at =dist<0= or null room.
- =propagate_message= (79) :: public entry: clears all rooms' visited flags via =hash_iterate(&room_db, clear_visited)=, marks the origin visited (occupants there are NOT messaged; the caller already used =act=), then =prop_mess= into each exit with =dist-1=. Leaves =visited= set on reached rooms, which =do_shout= then uses to avoid double delivery.
- =shoutf= (90) :: printf-style wrapper that formats into a =MAX_STRING_LENGTH= buffer and calls =do_shout(ch, buf, 0)=. Used by spec_mob.c and utils.c for mob shouts.
- =is_granted= (254) :: true if =cmd= (a =cmd_info= index) is in =ch->granted= or the account's =granted= =cmd_list= (=in_list=, wizard.c:3519). Callers in this file pass =-cmd=, which is the real index only for table entries with =num= 0 (chat, wiznet).
- =is_revoked= (263) :: same lookup against =ch->revoked= / account =revoked= lists.
- =channels[]= (824) :: static table {name, =PLR_= flag, reverse} driving =do_channel=: sports/=PLR_NOSPORTS=/reverse, auction/=PLR_AUCTION=, gossip/=PLR_GOSSIP=.

* cmds3.c — information and status (32 commands)

** page (=do_page_size=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:24
:MIN_POS: SLE
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: page report
: page width [width [min_width]]
: page height [height]
*** Parameters
- =report= :: (abbreviable) print current page height (default 22 when 0) and width/min-width (or "unlimited").
- =width [w [min]]= :: =one_argument= x2, numeric. No =w= shows current setting. =w= < =ABS_MIN_PAGE_WIDTH= (10) is rejected unless it is 0, which turns width wrapping off. =min= defaults to 0 and must be <= =w=.
- =height [h]= :: numeric; 0..255, 0 = default (22). Non-numeric rejected.
*** What it does
Configures the per-character pager: how many lines are sent before a "more" prompt and the column at which output is wrapped (with a minimum wrap column). With no argument prints a usage summary. Unknown subcommand prints "Type page with no arguments for correct syntax."
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->page_size=, =ch->page_width=, =ch->page_min_width=
- Writes :: =ch->page_size= (height), =ch->page_width=, =ch->page_min_width= (width; both zeroed when turned off)
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none.
- No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save (fields 102/103/105 in =player.save.c=, file =stash/<c>/<name>=).
- No log lines.

** kills (=do_kills=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:113
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: kills
: kills <player>            (level >= I8 only)
*** Parameters
- =player= :: =one_argument=; only honoured when =GET_LEVEL(ch) >= I8= (2008). Exact (=str_cmp=) name match against =character_list=, must be =CAN_SEE=. Otherwise the argument is ignored and the caller is the subject.
*** What it does
Shows the caller's (or, for high immortals, another character's) kill record: up to =MAX_KILL_SHOW= (20) entries of =v->kills[]= (=MAX_KILL_KEEP= = 125 slots) with vnum > 0, printing the mob's current name (or "No longer exists") and count. Computes a "rating" = number of listed entries + sum over entries of =get_mob_rank(mob) * MIN(30,nkills) / 1000=. If the caller stands in a leaderboard room (vnums 64491-64494, 3000, 1000 or 15) and this is a real command invocation, it instead prints the global top-10 kill table via =list_kills_table= and returns (the "Your ranking is: 0" line is written to a string block that is never flushed, so it is not shown; the table is not updated on this path). Is also invoked from =nanny.c:883= at login as =do_kills(ch,"",99)=: =cmd==99= suppresses all output and only refreshes the global table. After output, if =GET_LEVEL(ch) < 499= the caller's rating is written into the in-memory top-10 table via =update_kills_table(ch,ranking)= (note: uses =ch=, not the looked-up =v=).
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->in_room=, =v->kills[i].vnum/.nkills=, =character_list=, mob index via =real_mobp=, =get_mob_rank=, global =kill_table=
- Writes :: global =kill_table= (=kill_table.entry[]=, =.value[]=, =.num=) via =update_kills_table= for mortals < 499
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. =kill_table= is a global (=db.c:78=) reset to empty at boot and never written to disk; it is rebuilt as players log in.
- =ch->kills= itself is read-only here (it is saved by the routine player save as field 74).
- No log lines.

** users (=do_users=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:212
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: I1 (2001)
:END:
*** Syntax
: users [-c] [-t] [-n] [-l] [-h[hostprefix]] [-f] [-i] [-?]
*** Parameters
- =-n= / =-l= / =-h= / =-f= :: sort by name / level / host / descriptor id (=qsort= with =compare_name= etc.). =-hXXX= additionally filters to hosts that start with =XXX= (=is_abbrev=).
- =-t= :: totals only (no per-connection lines).
- =-i= :: immortals only (checks =d->original= or =d->character=).
- =-c= :: condensed two-column output.
- =-?= :: prints =USERS_HELP=.
- Options are only parsed when =GET_LEVEL(ch) >= I1= or the command was granted (=is_granted(ch,-cmd)=); a below-I1 caller who is not granted is forced to totals only.
*** What it does
Lists every connection in =connection_list=. For playing connections (=IS_CONN=) whose character the caller =CAN_SEE=: descriptor id, idle timer (capped 99), name, level, host, hit/hit_limit and the room (vnum, room name, zone name and number), or when switched, the mob being controlled. Non-playing connections (login/menu states) are listed with =describe_conn= and =connected_types[]= only when =GET_LEVEL(ch) >= I9= and not granted. Appends "Users: <visible>/<max_conn>/<player_count()>" for I1+ (non-granted) and the uptime since =boottime=. Output is paged.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =connection_list=, =d->identifier=, =d->host=, =d->connected=, =d->original=, =d->character=, =d->acct=, =c->timer=, =c->in_room=, =GET_HIT=, =hit_limit=, =world= via =get_rp=, zones via =get_zone_by_num=, =boottime=, =max_conn=, =ch->granted=/account granted lists
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** exits (=do_exits=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:348
:MIN_POS: RES
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: exits
*** Parameters
- none (argument ignored).
*** What it does
Walks the exit list of the caller's room and prints "Obvious exits:". Mortals skip exits to non-existent rooms, exits that are secret-and-closed or hidden, and see "Too dark to tell" for dark destinations; otherwise "<dir> - <room name>". Immortals see every exit with its exit number, destination vnum and room name, plus "*secret*" / "*locked*" markers and "swirling chaos of #N" for dangling exits. "(closed)" is appended for closed exits in both views. Prints "None." if nothing qualifies.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =get_rp(ch->in_room)->exits= (=room_exit=: =num=, =to_room=, =exit_info= flags =EX_SECRET/EX_CLOSED/EX_HIDDEN/EX_LOCKED=), =IS_DARK=, destination rooms
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** score (=do_score=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:497
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: score
*** Parameters
- none (argument ignored).
*** What it does
The main character sheet: age (birthday notice), remort count, hunger/thirst/drunk/fullness lines (mortal PCs with =CONFIG_HUNGER= set), hit/shield/mana/power/move and a class-dependent extra pool (rage for classes whose =build= contains "Wa" or "Bb", energy for "Th"; shield only if =hit_shield > 0=), alignment, questpoints and APEX points (shown if > 0, or immortal, or =remort_count > 5=), citizenship, clan rank and name, gold on hand and in bank, sex/race/class/level/exp line (hard-coded "Ladyboy" easter egg for the character named "Zurt"), exp to next level for mortals, name and title, play time, current zone (immortals also see zone number and minutes to reset), position, current opponent, away message, and for mobs their hate/fear lists. Finally calls =save_char(ch,NOWHERE)=, with the comment that this is meant to kick off PCs with null names.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->remort_count=, =ch->config_bits=, conditions (=GET_COND=, =GET_FULL=), =ch->hit_shield=, all point pools, =GET_ALIGNMENT=, =questpoints=, =apex_exp=, =ch->clan=/=get_clan_rank=, =GET_GOLD=/=GET_BANK=, =ch->sex=, race/class tables, =GET_EXP=, =EXP_NEEDED=, =ch->title=, =ch->time.logon/played=, zone of =ch->in_room=, =GET_POS=, =ch->fighting=, =ch->away=, =ch->hates/fears=
- Writes :: none in memory
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =save_char(ch,NOWHERE)= -> =stash/<first letter>/<name>= (every invocation).
- No log lines.

** wizhelp (=do_wizhelp=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:815
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: I1 (2001)
:END:
*** Syntax
: wizhelp
: wizhelp <immortal name>
: wizhelp <level>            (2001..2011)
*** Parameters
- =immortal name= :: rest of line, resolved with =get_char_vis=; that character's level and grant/revoke lists are used.
- =level= :: if no character matches, =atoi=; must be within =I1..I11= or usage is printed (usage text says 2001-2010). Numeric mode lists commands only, no grant/revoke section.
- Requested level above the caller's own is clamped back to the caller.
*** What it does
Lists privileged commands: every =cmd_info[]= entry with =I1 <= minimum_level <= vlevel=, minus any in the target's =revoked= list, sorted, 8 per line (12-char columns). For a named target (or self) it then appends "Granted Commands:" and "Revoked Commands:" sections drawn from both the character's =granted=/=revoked= lists and the account's (=ch_acct(vict)=), 7 per line.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =cmd_info[]=, =vict->granted/.revoked= (=list= arrays of command indices), account =granted/revoked=, =GET_LEVEL=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** info, finger (=do_info=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:1043
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: info <player>
: finger <player>
*** Parameters
- =player= :: rest of line, validated by =_parse_name= ("Illegal name." on failure). Looked up as an online PC in =character_list= (exact name); if absent, loaded from the player file with =get_pchar= (freed afterwards).
*** What it does
Player "finger": if the target is away and visible, shows the away message (and, for I5+, the account name and real name). Account name (I9+) and e-mail (I9+ or when asking about yourself) are shown when the account loads. Unless a mortal is asking about an immortal, shows first-login time (immortals only), then either "Last login on <time>" (offline or invisible target) or "On since <time>", with the last site appended for I5+. Ends with the target's =plan= or "No plan.". Reads the account record with =get_account(victim->account_name)= and frees it.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =character_list=, player file via =get_pchar=/=load_char=, account file via =get_account= (=a->last_site=, =a->real_name=, =a->email_address=, =a->name=), =victim->away=, =victim->first_login=, =victim->time.logon=, =victim->last_site=, =victim->plan=
- Writes :: none (temporary =char_data=/=acct_data= allocated and freed)
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change (reads =stash/<c>/<name>= and =account/<c>/<name>= when the target is offline).
- No log lines.

** who, whozone, iwho, clanwho, cwho (=do_who=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:1231
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: who 0 (mortal); whozone I10 (2010); iwho I4 (2004); clanwho/cwho 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: who
: who ?
: who <min-level> [max-level]
: who <player name>
: who arena | a
: who police | p
: who criminal | c              (level >= I6 or PLR_POLICE)
: who -class <class abbrev list>
: who -title <substring>
: iwho [same args]              (CMD_IWHO: ignores anonymity)
: clanwho|cwho [same args]      (CMD_CWHO: clan/rank columns)
: whozone [same args]           (CMD_WHO; identical to who)
*** Parameters
- =cmd= :: =CMD_WHO= (who, whozone), =CMD_IWHO= (sets =override_anon=), =CMD_CWHO= (sets =show_clan=). =override_anon= is also true for any caller above I5.
- =arg1= :: =one_argument=; "?" prints usage. Numeric (or empty) = min level; second numeric arg = max level (0 = unlimited). Non-numeric, non-keyword = a character name (=get_char_vis=), must be an online, non-linkdead PC.
- =-class= / =-title= :: rest of line matched against class abbreviations (=is_in_class_set=) or =v->title= (=str_str=).
*** What it does
Builds the player list from =connection_list= (playing connections only). Header depends on mode; arena mode prints the Blood Bath box (level range =chaos_lo/chaos_hi=, ticks to start/close, cost per level, pot) and lists contestants via =arena_who_string=, two per line. Otherwise each visible connection is filtered by the level range, class set, police flag, criminal status (=rent_disable > now=), title substring or the single looked-up name; anonymous (=PLR_ANON=) players are dropped whenever a level range is given. Invisible players are still listed when the viewer qualifies as a new player (=is_new_player=: viewer level <= 20, no remorts, non-PK clan) and the target's =invis_level <= 2000=. Each line is formatted by =format_who_string= (race-level-class tag, AVATAR/POLICE/immortal rank tag, name, title, =select_flags=) or =format_clan_string= in clan mode; switched immortals are listed as their original body. For a single looked-up player the e-mail is added for level >= 2009 and the away message if set. Ends with total players (level <= =MAX_MORT=) and immortals unless a single name was given. Paged.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =connection_list=, =d->character/original=, =v->act= (=PLR_ANON=, =PLR_POLICE=), =v->invis_level=, =v->title=, =v->email=, =v->away=, =v->rent_disable=, =v->clan/clan_rank=, class/race tables, arena globals =arena_ticks_till_start/close=, =arena_cost_per_level=, =arena_pot=, =chaos_lo/hi=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

*** What it does
Compact "short who": prints a Perilous Realms banner (or arena/police/criminal header), then visible, non-immortal, non-mob playing characters as names only, 7 per line; arena mode lists contestants regardless of visibility. Immortals seen are counted and listed separately under "Visible Immortals" with =imm_rank= titles, two per line. Ends with player and immortal totals. Paged.
*** Data touched

- Writes :: none

- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** news, oldnews, motd, credits, creators, wizlist, donations, contacts (=do_info2=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:1547
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: news
: oldnews
: motd
: credits | creators
: wizlist
: donations
: contacts
*** Parameters
- none from the player. The table entry's =data= field is a =char **= pointing at the boot-time text buffer (=&news=, =&oldnews=, =&motd=, =&credits=, =&wizlist=, =&donations=, =&services=). =cmd= arrives as the negated command index (since =num== 0) and is negated back to index =cmd_info[]=.
*** What it does
Pages the text file contents for the command that was typed. When the command is "motd" and the caller has an account, records the current time in =acct->motd=, which the login menu uses to decide whether to flag the MOTD as unread (=nanny.c:1081=). Prints "Sorry... temporarily unavailable." if the text pointer is null.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =cmd_info[cmd].cmd/.data=, the text globals
- Writes :: =ch->desc->acct->motd = time(0)= (motd only)
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. =acct->motd= is in memory; written as account field 10 on the next =save_acct= (logout, etc.) to =account/<c>/<name>=.
- No log lines.

** where, mwhere, pwhere (=do_where=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:1682
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: where I5 (2005); pwhere I4 (2004); mwhere I3 (2003)
:END:
*** Syntax
: where <object name>          (CMD_OWHERE)
: where <n>.<object name>
: mwhere <mob name>            (CMD_MWHERE)
: mwhere <n>.<mob name>
: pwhere [<player name>]       (CMD_PWHERE; no argument = all players)
*** Parameters
- =name= :: =only_argument= (whole line). Required except for =pwhere=. An optional numeric prefix must be written =n.name= ("If you are gonna use a number, use #.name"); it selects only the n-th match.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_OWHERE= / =CMD_MWHERE= / =CMD_PWHERE=; anything else prints "Mixup..".
*** What it does
Immortal locator. =where= scans =object_list= for objects whose =name= (keyword list) or =loaded_by_imm= contains the string, skipping objects ultimately carried by a character the caller cannot see; each hit is described by =describe_object= (index, coloured short description, vnum, location via =describe_obj_location=: room, carrier, container, warehouse). It then scans every object's =socket_apply[]= slots and lists socketed gems whose name matches (=describe_socketed_object=). =mwhere= scans =character_list= for mobs by =namelist=; =pwhere= for players by =name= (all players if no arg); both require =CAN_SEE= and print via =describe_char= (index, name, vnum for mobs, room name/vnum, zone, and host or <link-dead> for players). "Couldn't find any such thing." when nothing matches. Paged.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =object_list=, =obj->name=, =obj->loaded_by_imm=, =obj->in_obj/carried_by/equipped_by/in_room/in_warehouse/in_acct_warehouse=, =obj->socket_apply[]=, =character_list=, =c->namelist=, =c->in_room=, =c->desc->host=, rooms and zones
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** levels (=do_levels=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:1781
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: levels
: levels <lo> [<hi> [<class>]]
*** Parameters
- =lo= :: =one_argument=, =atoi=, must be > 0.
- =hi= :: optional, must be >= =lo=; defaults to =lo=. Mortals may not ask above 500.
- =class= :: rest of line, resolved with =which_class(ch,arg,1)= (lists classes on error); defaults to the caller's class.
*** What it does
With no argument: a level-500 mortal is told they are at the cap (and about remort if =remort_count < 6=); otherwise prints the experience still needed for the next three levels (skipping levels above 500 for mortals). With arguments prints, for each level in =[lo,hi]=, the =EXP_NEEDED= range for that level in the chosen class.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =GET_LEVEL=, =GET_EXP=, =GET_CLASS=, =ch->remort_count=, =classes[].multi= via =EXP_NEEDED=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** consider (=do_consider=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:1867
:MIN_POS: RES
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: consider <victim>
*** Parameters
- =victim= :: =only_argument=; =get_char_room_vis= (must be in the room and visible). Considering yourself prints "Easy! Very easy indeed!".
*** What it does
Estimates a fight. =set_mock= builds a mock (thac0 via =calc_thac0=, AC via =effective_ac= on body, hp, damage from strength/damroll/wielded weapon dice or mob dice) for both sides; each side's damage is reduced by half the other's head/body stopping power (min 1). Rounds-to-kill are computed both ways, scaled by hit probability derived from thac0-vs-AC and by relative =calc_speed=. The difference is bucketed with a level-dependent scale (5/7/10/20/25) into thirteen messages from "Too easy to be believed." to "You ARE mad!". Callers of level >= I5 or with =PLR_DEBUG= also see the raw AC/hp/dam/rounds/thac0 numbers and a numeric difficulty. Finally compares =HeightClass= of both and prints a size comparison.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: both characters' equipment (=WIELD=), =str_app[]=, =GET_DAMROLL=, =damnodice/damsizedice=, =stopping[]=, =GET_HIT=, speed, height
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

Level >= I9 additionally sees =mallinfo= memory figures (only when built with =MALLOP=) and total bytes sent (=meg_sent=/=data_sent=). Ends with registered player and account counts.
*** Data touched

- Writes :: none

** afflictions (=do_afflictions=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:3261
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: afflictions
: afflictions <target>         (immortals only; mortals' argument is ignored)
*** Parameters
- =target= :: whole argument passed to each helper, resolved with =get_char= (world-wide keyword lookup, no visibility check). Only honoured when =IS_IMMORTAL(ch)=; otherwise the caller is always the subject.
*** What it does
Prints everything currently modifying the subject, in order: remort bonuses (=get_remort_affects=: stat name, rank/max, bonus), skill-based affect bits such as sneaking/hiding/druid forms (=get_skill_affects=, only for immortals or =PLR_DEBUG=), spell and ability effects (=get_spell_affects=: =affected_by= bit flags, =affected= list, =affected_pulse= list, item-set abilities, berserk, silence/confusion), active item-set bonuses (=get_active_setbonuses=), and remaining ability, item-set and equipment cooldowns (=get_ability_cooldowns=). When an immortal names a target the headers read "<name> is affected by..." instead of "Affected by...".
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =remortInfo[]=/=nremort_bonuses=, =affected_by= bitfield, =affected= (=affected_type= list), =affected_pulse=, =affected_pulse_cooldown=, =item_set_ability=, =equipment[]= (=item_set=, =last_use_time=, =use_cooldown=, =rarity=), item-set tables, =apply_fields[]=
- Writes :: none (note: =get_spell_affects= =strdup='s set names into a local array and never frees them)
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** attribute (=do_attribute=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:3276
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: attribute
*** Parameters
- none (argument ignored; helpers are called with "").
*** What it does
Detailed physical/combat sheet: age in years and months, height, weight, carried weight vs capacity, iron weight worn/carried (only for classes flagged =FLAG_MAGE= or =FLAG_THIEF=), carried volume vs capacity, chosen true alignment, per-location armour class / stopping power, the ten magic resistances with base+bonus spell penetration, the seven stats, hitroll/damroll and attacks per second (=calc_speed_percent=; shown in red when exhausted, i.e. =GET_MOVE < 0= and level > 75), regeneration rates if any are non-zero, and any non-zero armour penetration, melee haste, life steal (Avengers always, including their passive), spell power, spell haste, spell vamp, assassinate bonus (if skill known) and backstab bonus summed from equipment applies and affects. Druids see their current shapeshift form. Then appends skill affects (immortals only), spell affects and active set bonuses for self. Level >= I2 also sees their building range (=build_lo=..=build_hi=) or a note that none is assigned.

** abort (=do_abort=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:3453
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: abort
*** Parameters
- none.
*** What it does
Prints "Command queue flushed." The actual flushing is done by the interpreter/queue: the table entry has priority 255 and =CMDF_FLUSH=, which makes =queue.c= discard queued lower-priority commands before this one runs.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: none
- Writes :: none here (the command queue is modified by =queue.c= because of the flag)
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

** ignore (=do_ignore=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:3458
:MIN_POS: RES
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: ignore
: ignore <player name>
*** Parameters
- =player name= :: rest of line; =get_char_vis_world= (must be online and visible). Rejected if a mob, an immortal, yourself, or already ignored (=is_ignoring_char=). Caller must have an account (=ch_acct=).
*** What it does
Account-level ignore. With no argument lists the character names currently ignored (the part of each entry after the tab) and hints at =unignore=. Otherwise records ="<victim account name>\t<victim char name>"= in the caller's account ignore list, so every character on that account is ignored.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =a->num_ignore=, =a->ignore_list[]=, =v->account_name=, =v->name=
- Writes :: =a->ignore_list= / =a->num_ignore= via =add_ignore=
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =add_ignore= calls =save_acct(a)= -> =account/<first letter>/<account name>= (ignore list is account field 17).
- No log lines.

** unignore (=do_unignore=)

:END:
*** Syntax
: unignore <player name>
*** Parameters
- =player name= :: rest of line, compared case-insensitively with the character-name part (after the tab) of each ignore entry. Caller must have an account. No argument prints usage or "You aren't currently ignoring anyone."
*** What it does
Removes the matching entry from the account's ignore list, or reports "You are not currently ignoring X".
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =a->ignore_list[]=, =a->num_ignore=
- Writes :: =a->ignore_list= / =a->num_ignore= via =remove_ignore=
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =remove_ignore= calls =save_acct(a)= -> =account/<c>/<account name>=.
- No log lines.

** spells (=do_spells=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: cmds3.c:3548
:MIN_POS: DEA
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: spells
: spells <class>
*** Parameters
- =class= :: rest of line, =which_class(ch,arg,1)=; defaults to the caller's class.
*** What it does
Lists the class's spell table (=classes[c].spells= linked list): index, name, minimum level, cost labelled "power" (=SOURCE_POWER=) or "mana", maximum learnable percentage and prerequisites (names, or "None"). When listing your own class the displayed cost is reduced by the remort efficiency bonus (=REMORT_POWER_EFF= / =REMORT_MANA_EFF= via =get_remort_bonus_with_multiplier=); other classes show the raw cost. Paged.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =classes[].spells= (=spell_entry=: =num=, =min_level=, =mana=, =max_learn=, =source=, =pre_reqs[]=), =spells[]= names, remort info
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.
- No log lines.

*** What it does
Lists the class's weapon proficiencies (entries in =classes[c].skills= with =num >= PROF_BASE=): index, a "<2h>" marker for two-handed weapon types (=weapons[].flags & WEAPF_TWO=), weapon type name, minimum level, max learn and prerequisite weapon types. Paged.
