** entrances (=do_entrances=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: builder.c:995
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I2 (2002)
:END:
*** Syntax
: entrances
*** Parameters
- none (argument ignored).
*** What it does
Walks every room in =room_db= (=hash_iterate= with =find_entrances=) and lists
each room that has an exit leading into the immortal's current room (vnum, name,
direction or named-exit keyword), plus rooms whose teleport target is this room.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =room_db=, =rp->exits=, =rp->tele_targ=, =ch->in_room=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.

** form (=do_form=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: builder.c:1004
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I2 (2002)
:END:
*** Syntax
: form <lo_room#> <hi_room#>
*** Parameters
- =lo hi= :: =sscanf("%d %d")=; both offset-adjusted when =cmd != -1=. Below I10, more than 200 rooms is refused (the surrounding condition is oddly nested so other bad ranges such as =hi<lo= just do nothing).
*** What it does
Bulk room creation: for every vnum in the range that does not yet exist,
=allocate_room=, zero it, set =number= and =zone=, name it after its vnum with
description "Empty". Both ends must be inside the builder's range
(=CHK_PLAYER_ZONE=, waived at I8+). Internal callers pass =cmd=-1= (wizard.c:384,
arena: rooms named "The killing fields.") or =cmd=-2= (wizard.c:4692, "The
Grudge Arena"); those skip the offset and range checks.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =room_db=, =ch->build_lo/hi=, =ch->offset=
- Writes :: new =room_data= entries in =room_db=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; rooms exist in memory only until =rsave=.

** mat (=do_mat=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: builder.c:1051
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I2 (2002)
:END:
*** Syntax
: mat <lo_room#> <hi_room#> <command>
*** Parameters
- =lo hi= :: two =one_argument= words, =atol=, offset-adjusted. A 0 becomes =OFFSET(ch,1)-1= (i.e. the builder's offset base). Range must be sane and at most 200 rooms; both ends checked with =CHK_PLAYER_ZONE= (unless =cmd==-1=).
- =command= :: rest of line.
*** What it does
"Multi-at": for every existing room in the range, calls =do_at(ch,"<vnum> <command>",cmd)=,
so the command is executed once in each room (e.g. =mat 1 50 redit rflags indoors=).
Each =do_at= applies its own permission checks.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =room_db=, =ch->build_lo/hi=, =ch->offset=
- Writes :: =ch->in_room= transiently via =do_at=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; depends on the embedded command.

** Helpers in builder.c
- =add_area= (23) / =boot_areas= (350) / =free_area_list= (49) :: load =lib/area.list= (=#define NAME offset= lines) into =area_list=, used by =room_number= to write =area:offset= references in text saves.
- =print_bitv= (61) :: print a bit vector as comma-separated names.
- =room_number= (76) :: render a vnum relative to the save base (or =area:n=).
- =write_string= / =write_string1= (101/116) :: quote strings for tran text format.
- =write_room= (130) :: write one room in tran text syntax (used by =save_range=).
- =purge_one_obj= (232) / =purge_one_room= (238) :: hash-iterate callbacks; the latter dumps people/objects into room 0 and deletes the room from =room_db=.
- =bin_write_room= (284) :: binary room record writer (id 255, tagged fields) used by =rsave=; counterpart of roomdb.c =read_one_room=.
- =save_range= (481) :: text-save a vnum range to =WORLD/<name>=.
- =save_world= (532) :: (not a command) writes =WORLD/world= plus one =WORLD/<area>= file per area in =area_list=.
- =boot_update= (923) :: at boot, loads every binary area file in =lib/UPDATE/= over the world (same reader as =rload=).
- =find_entrances= (976) :: callback for =do_entrances=.

* redit.c — in-game room editor
** rdig (=do_rdig=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: redit.c:588
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I2 (2002)
:END:
*** Syntax
: rdig <direction>
: rdig <exit keyword>
*** Parameters
- =direction= :: =dir_lookup=: n/e/s/w/u/d, nw/ne/se/sw (or full names), or a numeric exit number. Any other word is treated as a *named exit* keyword and gets the next free exit number (=find_unallocated_exit_num=).
*** What it does
Builder convenience: requires a build range and that the current room is inside
it. Finds the lowest vnum in =build_lo..build_hi= that has no room, creates that
room (zeroed, named after its vnum, "Empty"), links a new two-way exit from the
current room (refusing if an exit already exists in that direction, or if the
target already has a reverse exit / same-named exit), then =do_goto= into the
new room. Note: when the build range is completely full, =to_room= stays 0 and
the range check catches it; also if the new room is created but a later check
fails, the empty room is left allocated.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->build_lo/hi=, =ch->in_room=, =room_db=
- Writes :: new =room_data=; two =room_exit= records via =add_exit= (=num=, =to_room=, =key=-1=, =keyword=); =ch->in_room= via =do_goto=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; rooms/exits exist in memory only until =rsave=.

** edit, redit (=do_redit=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: redit.c:902
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: edit = I4 (2004); redit = I2 (2002)
:END:
*** Syntax
: redit <field> [args]
: redit                      (lists valid fields)
*** Parameters
- =field= :: prefix-matched against =field_type[]= (redit.c:78). Sub-commands (o-prefixed variants act on this side only; the plain form also updates the reverse exit in the far room):
  - =name [text]=, =description [text]= :: set string; with no text enters the '@'-terminated string editor (=ch->desc->str/max_str=, 80 / 4096 chars).
  - =rflags [set|clear] <bits..>|?= :: toggle/set/clear =rp->room_flags= from =room_bits=.
  - =sector_type <name>= :: choose from =sector_types=.
  - =river <dir> <speed>= :: =rp->river_dir/river_speed= (exit must exist).
  - =teleport <room> <time> <look>= :: =rp->tele_targ/tele_time/tele_look= (room offset-adjusted, -1 disables).
  - =exit|oexit <dir|keyword> <room|-1>= :: create (or delete with -1) an exit; target room offset-adjusted and must exist; =exit= also creates the reverse exit (range-checked).
  - =door|odoor <dir> <key> ['keywords'] [flags]= :: make exit a door: =key=, optional keywords, exit flags from =exit_bits= (=EX_ISDOOR= forced), copied to =exit_info=.
  - =exname|oexname <dir> <keywords>= :: exit keyword string.
  - =dflags|odflags <dir> [set|clear] <flags>|?= :: door flag bits (=default_state= and =exit_info=).
  - =exdesc <dir> [text]= :: exit =general_description= (string editor, 2048).
  - =extra <keyword>= :: add/replace an extra description (string editor, 4096).
  - =delete <keyword>= :: remove an extra description.
  - =max_tracks <n>= :: =rp->max_tracks= (-1 none, 0 default 3); frees =rp->track=.
  - =fix /search/replace= :: literal search-and-replace in the room description.
  - =dtime|odtime <dir> <time>= :: exit =reset_time= (BUG: tests =d= which is never assigned, so it always says "Create the exit first.").
  - =max_people <0..255>= :: =rp->max_people=.
  - =swap <extra1> <extra2>= :: swap order of two extras.
  - =proto <room#>= :: stores the prototype room number in a =_proto= extra (BUG: null check is on =rp= not =protoRP=, so a bad number dereferences NULL).
*** What it does
Edits the room the immortal is standing in. =CHK_PLAYER_ZONE(ch,ch->in_room)=
applies (below I8 the room must be inside =build_lo..build_hi=). Requires a
descriptor. Dispatches on the field index to the static =redit_*= helpers listed
above; string fields use the shared line editor in modify.c (=string_add=) by
pointing =ch->desc->str= at the target field.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->in_room=, =ch->build_lo/hi=, =room_db=, =room_bits=, =exit_bits=, =sector_types=
- Writes :: the current =room_data= (=name=, =description=, =room_flags=, =sector_type=, =river_*=, =tele_*=, =ex_description= list, =max_tracks=, =max_people=), =room_exit= records in this and the far room (=to_room=, =key=, =keyword=, =default_state=, =exit_info=, =reset_time=, =general_description=), =ch->desc->str/max_str=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; in-memory world edits only, written out by =rsave= (binary =Area/<name>=) or =tsave=.

** roomcopy (=do_room_copy=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: redit.c:961
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: roomcopy <from_room#> [<to_room#>]
*** Parameters
- =from_room= :: raw vnum (not offset-adjusted); must exist.
- =to_room= :: optional raw vnum; must exist, else defaults to the current room. Range-checked with =CHK_PLAYER_ZONE= (waived at I8+, i.e. always for this I9 command).
*** What it does
Copies name, description, room flags, sector type and a deep copy of the extra
descriptions from one room onto another. Exits, river, teleport, limits are not
copied. The destination's existing =ex_description= list is overwritten without
being freed when the source has extras (leak), and kept when the source has none.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: source =room_data=
- Writes :: destination =rp->name=, =description=, =room_flags=, =sector_type=, =ex_description=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; in-memory only until =rsave=/=tsave=.

** Helpers in redit.c
- =bit_vector= (16) :: parse a list of bit names against a name table with toggle/set/clear mode; '?' lists valid names.
- =list_list= (45) / =get_element= (105) :: print a name table; prefix-match one entry.
- =dir_lookup= (56) :: direction word or number -> exit number.
- =redit_string= (123) :: set a string or arm the '@' line editor on =ch->desc->str=.
- =redit_flags= / =redit_choose= (145/164) :: bit-vector and enum field editors.
- =redit_river=, =redit_teleport= (174/200) :: river and teleport fields.
- =redit_exit= (223) :: create/delete exits, optionally both directions.
- =redit_door=, =redit_exname=, =redit_dflags=, =redit_dtime= (360/447/482/870) :: door key/keywords/flags/reset time, optionally mirrored to the reverse exit found by =find_reverse_exit=.
- =redit_extra=, =redit_delete=, =redit_swap=, =redit_proto= (720/530/558/754) :: extra-description list maintenance.
- =redit_people=, =redit_tracks=, =redit_fix= (788/804/822) :: max_people, max_tracks, search/replace.

* objdb.c — object database, apply statistics, mutation, oedit
** objstats (=do_obj_stats=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: objdb.c:196
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: objstats
*** Parameters
- none.
*** What it does
Pages a table of the global =obj_apply_stats[]= gathered at boot (=update_obj_stats=
is called from =finish_obj= for every prototype): for each apply location the
count of prototypes carrying it, percentage of all applies, average/min/max
modifier (enum/bit applies show per-value sub-counts instead), followed by the
prototype total =obj_count=. This is the distribution =mutate_object= draws from.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =obj_apply_stats[]=, =obj_count=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.

** mutateobj (=do_mutate_object=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: objdb.c:546
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: mutateobj <object> <min_mutations>
*** Parameters
- =object= :: =one_argument=, found with =get_obj_vis= (inventory/equipment/room).
- =min_mutations= :: must start with a digit or nothing happens; passed as =min= to =mutate_object=.
*** What it does
Applies =min + roll_critical_chain(5,3)= random mutations to the object instance
(capped so applies fit in =MAX_OBJ_APPLY=): each picks an apply location
weighted by how common it is in the prototype database (=pick_random_apply=), a
modifier from that apply's observed range with random positive/negative
"karma" (=pick_modifier=, =validate_modifier_value= for enum types), and either
adds a new apply slot or adds to an existing one of the same location. Net
karma raises or lowers =o->rarity= (clamped COMMON..LEGENDARY unless already
GODLY+) and a net-negative result halves =min_level= if still at the prototype
value. =generate_short_description= rewrites the short description with
adjectives. If any mutation happened and the caller is immortal,
=obj->mutated_by_imm= is set to their name.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =obj_apply_stats[]=, prototype via =real_objp=
- Writes :: =obj->apply[]=, =obj->rarity=, =obj->min_level=, =obj->short_description=, =obj->mutation_count=, =obj->mutated_by_imm=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; the object instance is saved with whoever carries it on their next player/rent save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_DEBUG,"adding %d mutations to ...")= plus one =LOG_DEBUG= line per apply changed.

** oedit (=do_oedit=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: objdb.c:776
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I10 (2010)
:END:
*** Syntax
: oedit <object> list
: oedit <object> add <apply_name> <modifier | enum value>
: oedit <object> remove|rm|delete <apply description prefix>
: oedit <object> fix
*** Parameters
- =object= :: =one_argument=, =get_obj_vis=.
- =subcommand= :: prefix-matched: =add=, =remove=/=rm=/=delete=, =fix=, =list=. Anything else silently does nothing except the rarity clamp.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_OEDIT= (83), unused by the handler.
*** What it does
Per-instance apply editor. =list= prints each apply via =describe_apply=. =add=
matches =apply_name= by prefix against the apply schema names; enum/bit applies
take a value name (bit types XOR the bit in), numeric applies add =atoi(value)=
to an existing apply of that location or create a new slot; sets
=o->edited_by_imm=, bumps =mutation_count= and raises rarity on odd counts.
=remove= zeroes the first apply whose description starts with the argument and
lowers rarity on even counts. =fix= compares against the prototype and raises
any modifier that is worse than the prototype's (skipped entirely if the object
has mutations). Rarity is finally clamped to COMMON..LEGENDARY (GODLY+ preserved
inside add/remove).
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =obj_apply_stats[].apply_info= (Schema names/lists), prototype via =real_objp(obj->vnum)->obj=
- Writes :: =obj->apply[i].location/modifier/qualifier/args=, =obj->mutation_count=, =obj->rarity=, =obj->edited_by_imm=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; instance persists with its carrier's next player save.
- Log lines: below I11 every add/change is =vlog(LOG_DEBUG,"%s --- adding/changing ...")= (I11+ get it echoed instead); =fix_applies= logs each change at =LOG_DEBUG=.

** Helpers in objdb.c
- =apply_info_for_location= / =describe_apply= (38/48) :: schema lookup and "name by N" text for an apply.
- =init_apply_stats=, =update_apply_stats_with_modifier=, =update_obj_stats= (81/100/130) :: build =obj_apply_stats[]= from prototypes at boot.
- =print_apply_stats= (156) :: one table row for =objstats=.
- =pick_random_apply=, =pick_modifier=, =validate_enum_value=, =validate_modifier_value= (236/329/298/313) :: random mutation selection.
- =generate_short_description= (359) :: rebuild short description from mutations.
- =index_to_mutate= (436) :: find existing apply slot for a location.
- =mutate_object= (449) :: the mutation engine (also used by loot code).
- =oedit_list=, =oedit_remove=, =oedit_add=, =fix_applies= (565/575/601/685) :: oedit sub-commands.
- =reset_all_applies=, =reset_all_applies_keep_mutates= (717/738) :: copy prototype applies onto an instance (used by =resetplayer=).

* zone.c — zone reset tables and zone commands
** zreset (=do_zreset=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: zone.c:864
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: zreset
: zreset <zone#>
: zreset all
*** Parameters
- (none) :: zone containing the current room (=find_zone(ch->in_room)=).
- =zone#= :: =atoi=, zone number (1-based load order).
- =all= :: every zone; below I9 only zones fully inside =ch->test_lo..test_hi=.
*** What it does
Forces a zone reset now via =zreset(ch,z)= (zone.c:846): if the zone has
=boot_only= commands and no 2-element =save= range, those are run first, then
=reset_zone= runs the regular =cmds= list (=perform_commands=: mob/object loads
honouring zone limits, door states, etc.), sets =z->age=0= and clears limit
counters. Below I8, =CHK_PLAYER_CAN_DO_ZONE_COMMANDS= requires the zone to lie
inside the caller's test range.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =zone_list=, =Zone= fields, =ch->test_lo/hi=
- Writes :: world contents (mobs/objects loaded), =z->age=, =index_mem->zone_limit/zone_count= for =mob_db=/=obj_db=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; loaded mobs/objects live in memory (rooms in a zone's =save= range are picked up by the periodic =SaveZone= in world.save.c -> =WorldSave/zone.N=).
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_ZONE,"Reset zone %s")=.

*** Syntax

*** What it does
Despite the name, this does NOT write anything. It pages a reconstruction of
the zone's definition in zone-source syntax via =show_zone_details= (zone.c:1416):
name, =range=, optional =save= range (rooms persisted by world.save.c), =reset=
mode (never / ifempty / always, frequency), =boot_only {..}=, =zone_limit {..}=
and the =cmds= list, ending in =END=. Zone definitions themselves are only ever
read, from =lib/zone.out= by =ReadZones= (db.c:147); there is no in-game zone
writer.

- Writes :: none

** zpurge (=do_zpurge=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: zone.c:1675

:END:
*** Syntax
: zpurge
: zpurge <zone#>

*** Parameters
- (none) :: current room's zone; =zone#= :: =atoi=; =all= :: every room in =room_db= (I10+ only, otherwise falls to usage).
*** What it does
Runs =clear_room= (zone.c:1650) over every room in the zone's vnum range:
extracts every mob (=strip_char(ch,1)= + =extract_char=) and every object on the
floor (=extract_obj(obj,1)=), and tells any remaining players "The earth rumbles
and shakes." Players and their inventories are untouched. Below I8 the zone must
be inside the caller's test range.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =zone_list=, =room_db=
- Writes :: removes mobs/objects from the world
*** Persistence
- No direct save; in-memory only.

Lists zones with counts of visible players and mobs currently in each

** show (=do_show=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: show.c:663
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I4 (2004)
:END:
*** Syntax
: show shop [<shop#>]                                   (I5+)
: show worlds                                           (I5+)
: show events [removed|spell|strike|mobile|travel|point|object]   (I9+)
: show class [<abbrev>]                                 (I5+)
: show zonedetail [all|<zone#>|<search>]                (I9+)
: show zones [all|<name>]
: show objects <lo> <hi> [<search>|limited]             (I5+)
: show objects <search>|limited
: show rank <search> | show rank <lo> [<hi>] [<search>] (I5+)
: show mobiles <lo> <hi> [<search>|limited]             (I5+)
: show mobiles <search>|limited|kills
: show rooms <lo> <hi> [<search>] | show rooms <search>
*** Parameters
- =what= :: first word, abbreviation-matched in the order above; the level guards are per sub-command. NPCs are refused.
*** What it does
Umbrella read-only browser. =shop= lists shops or one shop's keeper/room/wares/hours.
=worlds= dumps the =worldzone[]= table. =events= lists the short-term
=event_queue= (spells, strikes, mob actions, travel, updates). =class= prints
class definitions. =zonedetail= is =show_zone= (same output as =zfile=).
=zones= is =show_zones= with all zones and the wide format. =objects=/=mobiles=
browse =obj_db=/=mob_db= by vnum range with optional name search, =limited=
(max_exist < 32000 with counts) or, for mobs, =kills= (sorted by =num_destroyed=).
=rank= lists mobs with their computed rank/XPR. =rooms= browses =room_db= by
range or name, flagging blank vnum stretches.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =shop_index=, =worldzone[]=, =event_queue[]=, =classes[]=, =zone_list=, =obj_db=, =mob_db=, =room_db=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.

* utils.c — player item reset and world balance statistics
** resetplayer (=do_resetplayer=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: utils.c:1537
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I10 (2010)
:END:
*** Syntax
: resetplayer <name> eq|inv|bank|abank|all soft|hard
*** Parameters
- =name= :: visible player (=get_char_vis=).
- =scope= :: =eq= (worn), =inv= (carried), =bank= (=victim->warehouse=), =abank= (account warehouse), =all=.
- =soft|hard= :: mandatory. =hard= uses =reset_one_item= (full reset to prototype); =soft= uses the =_keep_mutates= variants that keep mutation gains.
- Note the handler signature is =(char_data *ch, char *args)= — no =cmd= parameter; also called from wizard.c:3356.
*** What it does
Walks the chosen item lists and calls the per-item reset functions, which
overwrite the instance's applies (and related fields) from the prototype via
=reset_all_applies= / =reset_all_applies_keep_mutates=. When a reset function
returns 1 the item is judged invalid (e.g. prototype gone or excluded) and is
removed from wherever it is and =extract_obj='d, reporting "Deleting object".
After equipment resets, =reset_affects=/=reset_affected_by= recompute the
victim's stats. For =abank= the account is taken from =ch_acct(victim)= or
loaded from disk with =load_acct=, then =save_acct= and freed.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =victim->equipment[]=, =victim->carrying=, =victim->warehouse=, account =warehouse=, object prototypes
- Writes :: object =apply[]= etc. on every item; items may be extracted; victim affects recomputed; account warehouse
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =save_char(victim, NOWHERE)= -> =lib/stash/<c>/<name>= always at the end; =save_acct(acc)= -> =lib/account/<c>/<name>= in the =abank=/=all= path.
- Log lines: the reset helpers may =vlog(LOG_DEBUG, ...)= per item.

** worldstats (=do_get_world_stats=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: utils.c:2329
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I8 (2008)
:END:
*** Syntax
: worldstats exphigh|explow|goldhigh|goldlow|rank <zone#>|all
*** Parameters
- =mode= :: =exphigh=/=highexp=, =explow=/=lowexp=, =goldhigh=/=highgold=, =goldlow=/=lowgold=, =rank=.
- =zone#|all= :: mandatory; zone number compared against the mob's =default_zone= (mobs with no zone are skipped unless =all=).
- Handler signature is =(char_data *ch, char *arg)= — no =cmd=.
*** What it does
Balance audit over every *loaded* mob (=character_list=, level 30..500, not in the
hard-coded =ignored_mobs[]= list, rank <= 15M). Compares each mob's gold with
=get_mob_gold_ratio= and exp with =get_mob_xpr=; lists those more than 10% below
(=..low=) or above (=..high=) the formula, with suggested values, then totals.
=rank= prints every mob's level and =get_mob_rank_string= guess. Mobs with
gold between 2 and 299 are skipped in the gold modes.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =character_list=, =GET_GOLD=, =GET_EXP=, =mob->default_zone=, =zone_list=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.

** Helpers in utils.c
- =do_resetobject= (1496) :: single-object variant (registered elsewhere).
- =reset_one_item= / =reset_one_item_keep_mutates= (2073/1788) :: reset one carried/worn item to prototype; return 1 if the item should be deleted.
- =reset_one_item_account_bank= / =_keep_mutates= (2176/1935) :: same for account-warehouse items.
- =reset_items_in_container= (702) :: recurse into container contents (hard =abank= path).
- =get_long_number_formated= / =get_number_formated= (2302/2315) :: thousands-separated numbers for the report.
- =MoveTo= (351) :: see =do_moveto= below.

* modify.c — object string editor
** string (=do_string=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: modify.c:130
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I6 (2006)
:END:
*** Syntax
: string <object> name|short|long <text>
: string <object> description <keyword>        (then '@'-terminated editor)
: string <object> delete-description <keyword>
: string <object> value <gold>                 (I8+)
: string <object> owner [<text>]               (I8+)
: string <object> rarity <0..254>              (I8+)
: string <object> mutation <0..254>            (I8+)
: string <object> min_level <0..500>           (I10+)
: string <object> clearedited 1                (I11+)
: string <object> sockets <n>                  (I11+)
*** Parameters
- =object= :: =get_obj_vis=. Despite the field table mentioning mobs (=TP_MOB=), only objects are handled.
- =field= :: matched by =old_search_block= against =string_fields[]= (name, short, long, description, title, delete-description, value, owner, rarity, mutation), or the literal words =min_level=, =clearedited=, =sockets= (numeric argument required).
- =text= :: rest of line; for name/short/long/owner an empty text enters the '@' editor. Max lengths from =length[]=: name 25, short 85, long 256, ex-desc 240, owner 85.
*** What it does
Immortal object re-stringing. Sets =ch->desc->str= to the chosen field, frees the
old value, and either stores the truncated text at once or arms the line
editor (=string_add=). =description= adds/replaces an extra description keyed by
=keyword= (always via the editor). =value= sets =obj->cost= (1..9999998),
=rarity=/=mutation= set =obj->rarity=/=mutation_count=, =min_level= sets
=obj->min_level=, =sockets= sets =obj->sockets=, =clearedited= wipes the
=stringed_by_imm=/=loaded_by_imm=/=edited_by_imm= audit names. Text and
min_level edits stamp =obj->stringed_by_imm=; sockets stamps =edited_by_imm=.
Field 5 (=title=) falls to "undefined for objects".
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->desc=, object fields
- Writes :: =obj->name/short_description/description/owner=, =obj->ex_description=, =obj->cost=, =obj->rarity=, =obj->mutation_count=, =obj->min_level=, =obj->sockets=, =obj->stringed_by_imm=, =obj->loaded_by_imm=, =obj->edited_by_imm=, =ch->desc->str/max_str=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; object persists with its carrier's next player save.
- Log lines: every invocation is =slog("STRING(<imm>): <args>")=.

** Helpers in modify.c
- =string_add= (54) :: the shared '@'-terminated multi-line editor: appends input to =*d->str=, enforces =d->max_str=, fires =d->character->funct= when done. Also used by =redit= and boards.
- =quad_arg= (110) :: split "name field text" for =do_string=.

* ruby_cmds.c — Ruby eval hook (compiled out)
** ` (=do_ruby_eval=) -- NOT REGISTERED on Linux builds
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: ruby_cmds.c:17
:MIN_POS: n/a  (DEA when built with Ruby)
:MIN_LEVEL: n/a  (I9 when built with Ruby)
:END:
*** Syntax
: ` <ruby expression>
*** Parameters
- =expression= :: rest of line, handed to =rb_eval_string_protect=.
*** What it does
Inert. The cmd_info entry (h/inter.h:407-408, command name backquote) is inside
=#ifndef NO_RUBY=, =makefile.linux= defines =-DNO_RUBY= and does not even list
=ruby_cmds.o= in =OBJS=, so on Linux the command does not exist. When built with
Ruby it would: =dup= stdout, =dup2= the player's socket fd onto fd 1, evaluate
the string in the embedded Ruby interpreter, =rb_p= the result (or
=rb_backtrace= on error) so it appears on the player's connection, then restore
stdout. Full arbitrary code execution as the game process.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->desc->identifier=
- Writes :: process fd 1 (temporarily); anything the Ruby code does
*** Persistence
- None on Linux (dead code). With Ruby: arbitrary.

*** Syntax

*** Parameters

*** What it does
Chooses which =vlog(LOG_xxx,...)= categories are echoed to this immortal in

** config (=do_config=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: log.c:131
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: config
: config <option>
*** Parameters
- =option= :: abbreviation of =config_bits[]=: map, manualunlock, autosplit, shortlook, timestamps, keywords (mortal); feelhunger, channames (I1+).
*** What it does
Player preference toggles stored in =ch->config_bits= (bit index = table
position). Toggling =feelhunger= (index 6, =CONFIG_HUNGER=) also resets the
hunger/thirst/drunk conditions and fullness: turning it *off* (bit currently
set) writes -1 to all (never hungry); turning it on sets them to 10, then
=update_pos=. Always prints the full table afterwards with yes/no and, for the
toggled line, the old -> new state.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->config_bits=, =GET_LEVEL(ch)=
- Writes :: =ch->config_bits=; =ch->conditions[HUNGER/THIRST/DRUNK]=, =ch->fullness= (feelhunger only)
*** Persistence
- No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save (=config_bits= field 118).

** Helpers in log.c
- =log_bits[]= / =config_bits[]= (11/117) :: name, min level (and description) tables that define the bit numbering.
- =transaction= (100) :: (not used by these commands) account money change with =LOG_ACCOUNT= line and =save_acct=/=save_char=.

* ticks.c — pulse tuning and timers
** ticks (=do_ticks=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: ticks.c:38
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: ticks show
: ticks set <field> <value>
: ticks reset <field>|all
*** Parameters
- =field= :: abbreviation of =tickfield[]=: timeout, zone, mobile, river, audio, teleport, violence, fall, drown, player, healer, tics, pulse, maxpulse, all.
- =value= :: =atol=, must be within the field's min/max (1..32000 for most; timeout 0..2^30-1; tics/pulse unbounded). =set all= is refused.
*** What it does
Live tuning of the heartbeat: =set= assigns the corresponding global
(=PULSE_ZONE=, =PULSE_MOBILE=, =PULSE_VIOLENCE=, =PULSE_PLAYER=, ... =TICS=,
=PULSE=, =PULSE_MAX=), =reset= restores the =DEF_= default (=reset all= calls
=tick_reset=, which also resets =PULSE_ZONE_SAVE=, =PULSE_PROMPT=,
=PULSE_ABILITY=, =PULSE_MODERN_TIC=), =show= prints current vs default for every
field. Lower values make that subsystem run more often.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =tickfield[]=, the =PULSE_*= globals
- Writes :: =PULSE_TIMEOUT=, =PULSE_ZONE=, =PULSE_MOBILE=, =PULSE_RIVER=, =PULSE_AUDIO=, =PULSE_TELEPORT=, =PULSE_VIOLENCE=, =PULSE_FALL=, =PULSE_DROWN=, =PULSE_PLAYER=, =PULSE_HEALER=, =TICS=, =PULSE=, =PULSE_MAX= (and more via =tick_reset=)
*** Persistence
- No direct save; process globals only, lost on reboot.

** timer (=do_timer=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: ticks.c:219
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: timer
: timer 1
: timer 0
*** Parameters
- =1= / =0= :: enable / disable profiling; anything else prints usage (which wrongly says "ticks [0 | 1]").
*** What it does
With no argument, prints the accumulated wall-clock time spent in each
heartbeat subsystem (zone update, mobile activity, river, audio, teleport,
violence, player tick with weather/affect/point breakdown, game loop) from the
=TIMER_*= accumulators, plus whether the timer is on. =1=/=0= sets the global
=TIMER= flag that gates the accumulation.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =TIMER=, =DIFF_TIMER(TIMER_*)= accumulators
- Writes :: =TIMER=
*** Persistence
- No direct save; process globals only.

** Helpers in ticks.c
- =tick_usage=, =tick_reset=, =tick_show= (156/168/190) :: usage text, restore all defaults, current/default table.
- =timer_usage=, =timer_reset=, =timer_show= (235/240/258) :: usage, zero the =TIMER_*= accumulators (called at boot), print the table.

* events.c — long-term scheduled events
** events (=do_events=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: events.c:178
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: events
*** Parameters
- none.
*** What it does
Pages the long-term =event_list= (things queued with =schedule()=: room-flag
restores, char callbacks, function calls, object callbacks, rescheduled
short-term events) showing the current tick and each event's id, due tick and
type name. This is distinct from the per-pulse =event_queue= shown by
=show events=.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =event_list=, =current_tick=, =event_types[]=
- Writes :: none
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.

** cancel (=do_cancel=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: events.c:200
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: cancel <event_id>
*** Parameters
- =event_id= :: =atoi=, positive; ids are shown by =events=.
*** What it does
Unlinks the event with that id from =event_list= and frees it, so its callback
never runs (e.g. a door that would re-lock or a room flag that would clear stays
as is). Reports "Couldn't find event" otherwise.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =event_list=
- Writes :: =event_list= (node removed and freed)
*** Persistence
- No direct save; in-memory only.

** Helpers in events.c
- =schedule= (61) :: varargs constructor inserting an =event= sorted by due tick into =event_list=; types =EV_FUNC=, =EV_ROOM_FLAG=, =EV_CHAR_DATA=, =EV_OBJECT=, =EV_RESCHED=.
- =validate_char= / =validate_obj= (124/131) :: linear checks that a pointer is still in =character_list=/=object_list= before firing.
- =event_tick= (138) :: called once per tick; fires all due events.
- =free_event_queue=, =clean_event_queue= (227/246) :: shutdown and =EVT_REMOVED= compaction of the short-term =event_queue[]=.

* stat.c — stat family
** stat, mstat, pstat, rstat, astat, whois (=do_stat=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: stat.c:1312
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: stat I5 (2005); mstat I4 (2004); pstat I5 (2005); rstat I2 (2002); astat I9 (2009); whois I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: rstat [<room#> | extras]
: stat [<n>.]<object keyword>
: stat <player name>              (falls back to listing that player's equipment)
: mstat [<n>.]<mob keyword>
: pstat [<n>.]<player keyword>
: astat [<n>.]<account or player name>
: whois [<n>.]<account or player name>
*** Parameters
- =cmd= :: selects the family: =CMD_RSTAT= (48), =CMD_OSTAT= (40), =CMD_MSTAT= (41), =CMD_PSTAT= (42), =CMD_ASTAT= (49), =CMD_WHOIS= (66).
- =n.= :: optional ordinal prefix ("2.sword"); a bare number without '.' is rejected.
- =room#= :: offset-adjusted; you must pass =allowed_into_room= (no PRIVATE, LORD_ONLY below I9, etc.).
*** What it does
A guard at the top refuses anyone below level 2001 unless their account is
"raymond" (a hard-coded exception). =rstat= dumps the current or given room
(=stat_room=), or just its extra descriptions with =extras=. =stat= searches the
global =object_list= by name substring for the n-th match and calls =stat_obj=;
if nothing matches it instead prints every object equipped by a player of that
name. =mstat=/=pstat= search =character_list= for the n-th visible mob / player
whose namelist contains the text (=stat_char=). =astat= and =whois= look up an
account by name via =get_account_char= (=whois= gives the short form), falling
back to the account of a matching online player.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =object_list=, =character_list=, =room_db=, account files via =get_account_char=/=ch_acct=, =ch->account_name=
- Writes :: none (account structs loaded from disk are freed)
*** Persistence
- Read-only; no state change.

** Helpers in stat.c
- =get_account_char= (22) :: find the n-th account matching a name (loads from disk).
- =stat_account= (49), =stat_room= (143), =stat_char= (228), =stat_obj= (842) :: the formatters for each family.

* spec_mob.c — path-finding test command
** moveto (=do_moveto=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: spec_mob.c:6276
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: moveto <room#>
*** Parameters
- =room#= :: =atoi(arg)=, raw vnum (no offset); 0 prints usage.
*** What it does
Debug hook for the mob travel system: calls =MoveTo(ch,dest)= (utils.c:351),
which runs =setup_path= from the caller's room to the destination and, if a path
exists, queues an =EVT_TRAVEL= event so the caller walks there step by step
(=event_move=), the same mechanism hunting/traveling mobs use. Then prints the
computed room-by-room path from =ch->dest_room[]=, or "Apparently there is no
path." Immortals are not mobs, so the =ACT_STAY_ZONE=/=ACT_TRAVELING= parts are
skipped.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =room_db= (path search), =ch->in_room=
- Writes :: =ch->dest_room[]= (allocated path), =ch->dest_room_i=; a travel event in =event_queue=; later =ch->in_room= as the event walks the path
*** Persistence
- No direct save; in-memory only.

* obj1.c — picking up, dropping, handing over objects and gold

** socket (=do_socket=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj1.c:241
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 1
:END:
*** Syntax
: socket insert <gem> <item>
: socket combine <gem>
: socket remove <item> <id>
: socket destroy <item> <id>
: socket info <item>
*** Parameters
- =subcommand= :: first of =three_argument_interpreter=; must =isname= one of =insert= =combine= =remove= =destroy= =info=, else usage text.
- =gem= / =item= :: object keywords, looked up with =get_obj_in_list_vis= in =ch->carrying= only (equipment lookup is commented out) except =info=, which falls back to =get_obj_vis_equ=.
- =id= :: =atoi=; 1-based index of the socket group on the item (groups = distinct =socket_apply[].identifier=). Out of range prints the valid ids.
*** What it does
=combine=: the gem's =value[0]= is how many copies are needed and =value[1]= the result vnum; if =obj_count_list(ch->carrying, vnum)= is enough, =combine_socket= (handler.c) loads the result with =get_obj(value[1],0)=, gives it to the player (1.5 s =WAIT_STATE=) and =remove_obj_count_list= destroys =value[0]= copies. =info=: for an =ITEM_SOCKET= gem it runs =do_examine= and (mortals only) prints the removal cost from =get_and_set_gem_removal_costs= (tiered by =obj_tier=: 1k gold at tier 1 up to 8M gold + 500 apex per tier at 15+); for a socketed item it lists each gem group with its affects, removal cost (floored at 1000 gold, coloured by affordability) or "not removable". =remove=: =remove_socket_from_item(...,0)= refuses non-removable gems, checks =ch->gold=/=ch->apex_exp= against the cost, rebuilds =socket_apply[]= without the group, returns a fresh copy of the gem to inventory, charges the cost and waits 3 s. =destroy=: same rebuild with no cost and no gem back. =insert=: item needs =->sockets > 0=, gem must be =ITEM_SOCKET=; gem =value[3]= (unique) refuses a second copy already on that item (=has_unique_gem=); full when =socket_count(item) == item->sockets=; =add_socket_to_item= copies each gem =apply[]= into =item->socket_apply[]= (identifier = =time(0)=, removable = gem =value[2]=, unique = gem =value[3]=), extracts the gem, 3 s wait.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, =obj->value[0..3]=, =obj->sockets=, =obj->socket_apply[]=, =obj->obj_tier=, =ch->gold=, =ch->apex_exp=, =IS_IMMORTAL(ch)=.
- Writes :: =item->socket_apply[]=, =ch->gold -=…=, =ch->apex_exp -=…=, objects created/extracted in =ch->carrying=, =WAIT_STATE(ch)=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted (objects, gold, apex) by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_DEBUG)= when a result/gem prototype cannot be loaded.

** get, take, getnodrop (=do_get=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj1.c:492
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: get <obj> | all.<obj> | <N>*<obj>
: get all
: get <obj> | all.<obj> | <N>*<obj> <container>
: get all <container>
: get all corpses
: getnodrop <obj> [<container>]
*** Parameters
- =arg1 arg2= :: =argument_interpreter= (two lowercased words). =all.x= via =getall= means unlimited (=num=-1=); =N*x= via =getabunch= means up to N (capped at 9); otherwise one.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_GET= (77) for =get=/=take=; =CMD_GET_NODROP= (82) for =getnodrop=, which is the only way to pick up an unbound =ITEM_NODROP= object and which refuses every =all= form ("Don't be a dweeb!").
*** What it does
Dispatches on the argument shape: none → "Get what?"; =all= → every visible object in the room; =<x>= → objects in the room matching the name; =all all= → joke refusal; =all <c>= → everything in container =c= (=get_obj_vis_accessible=: inventory first, then room; must be =ITEM_CONTAINER= or =ITEM_SPELL_POUCH=); =<x> all= → refused; =<x> <c>= → matching objects from container =c=; =all corpses= → walks every container in the room. Room 99 refuses =get all= for levels below =I1=. Before each object: trap checks (=CheckForAnyTrap=/=CheckForGetTrap=/=CheckForInsideTrap= → =TriggerTrap=), =corpse_consent= (containers with =held_for= need the owner's =CONSENT= unless same name, level ≥ =I9=, world zone ≠ 0, or an uncharmed NPC; =PK_LOOT= corpses need a =CLAN_PKCLAN=/=CLAN_PKOTHERS= clan), =can_carry= (volume/weight limits, then =can_shift=: =ITEM_TAKE=, insurance token vnum 25, =can_loot= nodrop ownership, =can_have= skill/limit — I9 on either side bypasses) and =can_get_unbound_nodrop= (immortals always; NPCs never; owned NODROP only by its owner). =phys_get= moves the object (=obj_from_room=/=obj_from_obj= → =obj_to_char=), calls =SaveRoom= when taken from a container, and converts =ITEM_MONEY= into =ch->gold= (auto-split to the group when =CONFIG_AUTO_SPLIT= and not from a corpse) then extracts it. In the =<x> <c>= case a NODROP that cannot be taken but passes =can_shift= is instead shifted to the ground (=phys_shift=). If an object lookup fails after at least one success the loop ends silently.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =rp->contents=, =container->contains=, =container->value[1]= (=CONT_CLOSED=), =obj->extra_flags= (=ITEM_NODROP=), =obj->owner=, =obj->held_for=, =ch->config_bits=, =GET_WORLDZONE(ch)=, =get_clan(ch)->flags=, =HAS_CONSENT=.
- Writes :: object lists (=ch->carrying=, room/container contents), =ch->gold +=…= for money, =obj->in_room= repair for orphaned objects, gold of group members via =raw_split=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =phys_get= → =SaveRoom(ch->in_room)= after taking from a container (room.save.c writes =RoomSave/<vnum>.room= only if the room has the =AUTOSAVE= flag). Otherwise in-memory; player inventory/gold persisted by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =slog("OBJ:%s got %s from room -1...")= when an object's =in_room= was =NOWHERE=; =vlog(LOG_URGENT)= from =can_shift= if a limit-owner lookup fails.

** drop (=do_drop=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj1.c:788
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: drop <obj>
: drop <N> <obj>
: drop <N>*<obj> | all*<obj>
: drop all
: drop <N> coins|coin
*** Parameters
- =arg= :: =only_argument= (rest of line). A =*= splits a count from the name (=all*= = 32000); a leading number followed by a space is also a count (both must be positive). Names containing =.= skip the leading-number parse.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_DROP= (45), unused.
*** What it does
Coins: needs at least 25 and no more than =GET_GOLD(ch)=; immortals between =MAX_MORT= and =I9= are refused and logged; otherwise =create_money(num)= is placed in the room and =ch->gold -= num=. =drop all=: refused for auctioneers (logged) and when carrying nothing; each carried object passing =CAN_DROP= (mortals: not =ITEM_CURSED=/=ITEM_TEST=/=ITEM_NODROP=, never vnum 25, no container holding an owned NODROP except vnum 0) is moved to the room. A named object repeats =phys_drop= =num= times (stops when the same object is found twice, i.e. no more matches); =phys_drop= lets level ≥ =I9= drop anything and logs =LOG_URGENT= if an auctioneer is made to drop. For mortals in rooms without =AUTOSAVE= the dropped object gets =timer2 = DROP_TICKS= (30) or ×200 for NODROP so it decays.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, =GET_GOLD(ch)=, =GET_LEVEL(ch)=, =obj->extra_flags=, =obj->contains=, =ROOM_FLAG(ch->in_room,AUTOSAVE)=, =is_auctioneer(ch)=.
- Writes :: =ch->gold -= num=, =obj->timer2=, object moved =obj_from_char= → =obj_to_room=, new money object.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none (no =SaveRoom= here; an =AUTOSAVE= room is written by other paths). No direct save; in-memory only, player side persisted by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_URGENT)= for mid-level immortals dropping coins, for auctioneer =drop all=, and in =phys_drop= when an auctioneer drops.

** put (=do_put=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj1.c:898
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: put <obj> <container>
: put all.<obj> <container>
: put <N>*<obj> <container>
*** Parameters
- =arg1= :: object in inventory (=generic_find FIND_OBJ_INV=); =all.x= → unlimited, =N*x= → up to 9; a bare =all= is refused.
- =arg2= :: container, found in inventory first, then inventory-or-room (=FIND_OBJ_INV|FIND_OBJ_ROOM= → =get_obj_vis_accessible=).
- =cmd= :: =CMD_PUT= (78), unused.
*** What it does
Loops while =num != 0=. If the container is not carried, mortals may not put an =ITEM_NODROP= object, or a container holding one, into it. The container must be =ITEM_CONTAINER= or =ITEM_SPELL_POUCH= (pouches accept only =is_spell_component= vnums for the player's class or =ITEM_SPELL_GEM=). Mortals cannot put cursed, test, or insurance-token (vnum 25) items in; corpses never fit; the container must be open (=CONT_CLOSED= clear), not the object itself, and =OBJECT_FITS_IN_OBJECT=. On success the object moves =obj_from_char= → =obj_to_obj= and, if the container is in a room, =SaveRoom(ch->in_room)=. Putting into an auctioneer's container is logged. Note =generic_find= with =FIND_OBJ_INV|FIND_OBJ_ROOM= always returns =FIND_OBJ_INV=, so the =obj_from_room= branch is dead code.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, room contents, =obj->extra_flags=, =obj->contains=, =container->value[1]=, =container->in_room=, =IS_IMMORTAL(ch)=.
- Writes :: object moved into =container->contains=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =SaveRoom(ch->in_room)= when the container is in a room → =RoomSave/<vnum>.room= (only for =AUTOSAVE= rooms). Otherwise in-memory, persisted with the player's inventory.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_URGENT,"Someone is making an auction grab bag ...")=.

** pay (=do_pay=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj1.c:1032
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: I9 (2009)
:END:
*** Syntax
: pay <amount> <player>
*** Parameters
- =amount= :: =one_argument=; must satisfy =is_number=, be > 0.
- =player= :: =one_argument=; =get_char_room_vis=, must be a PC other than the payer.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_PAY= (93), unused.
*** What it does
Immortal-only (registered at =I9=) gold transfer. Refuses if the payer lacks the gold and is an NPC or below =I9= (so I9+ never fails this check). Deducts =amount= from the payer only when NPC or below =I5=; always adds it to the victim. Messages to both and the room; logs transfers above 100,000,000. The =!*argument= check happens after the first word is removed, so a missing recipient gives "Pay how much to who?". The victim is looked up twice (redundant).
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =GET_GOLD(ch)=, =GET_LEVEL(ch)=, room people.
- Writes :: =ch->gold -= amount= (conditional), =vict->gold += amount=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save of each character.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_MISC,"%s paid %s coins to %s")= when amount > 100,000,000.

** give (=do_give=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj1.c:1106
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: give <N> coins|coin|gold <char>
: give <obj> <char>
: give all.<obj> <char>
: give <N>*<obj> <char>
*** Parameters
- =obj_name= :: =one_argument=; if numeric the next word must be =coins=/=coin=/=gold=.
- =vict_name= :: =one_argument=; =get_char_room_vis=, not self.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_GIVE= (44), unused.
*** What it does
Gold: amount > 0, payer must have it unless level ≥ =I9=; deducted only from NPCs and players below =I5=; logged above 2,000,000. Objects: for each match in =ch->carrying= (=all.=/=N*= forms), =can_carry(ch,vict,obj)= applies the receiver's volume/weight and =can_shift= rules (messages go to both). An awake humanoid NPC (not charmed by a player, and the giver not a player-controlled mob) refuses armour/weapons it rates 0 (=rate_armor=/=rate_weapon=) or cannot see, unless the giver is immortal. Mortals cannot give =ITEM_CURSED=/=ITEM_TEST=; a NODROP older than =NODROP_TRANSFER_WINDOW= (24 h from =created_date=) cannot change hands unless one side is immortal, and non-corpse containers holding such an item are refused. Immortals below =I11= giving items rarer than =RARITY_RARE= are logged. Then =obj_from_char= → =obj_to_char(vict)=.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =GET_GOLD(ch)=, =GET_LEVEL(ch)=, =ch->carrying=, =obj->extra_flags=, =obj->created_date=, =obj->owner=, =obj->rarity=, =vict->master=, =AFF_CHARM=, =IsHumanoid(vict)=.
- Writes :: =ch->gold=, =vict->gold=, object ownership lists.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_MISC)= for gold > 2,000,000 and for immortal (< =I11=) gifts of rare+ items.

** Helpers in obj1.c
- =can_carry= (17) :: volume/weight check for receiver =ch= (bypassed if either side ≥ =I9=), then =can_shift=.
- =can_shift= (48) :: legality of moving =obj= to =ch=: =ITEM_TAKE=, insurance token vnum 25, =can_loot= nodrop ownership, =can_have= level/skill/max-per-player limit (with location hint for immortals).
- =phys_shift= (111) :: move an object from an open container to the room floor with messages.
- =phys_get= (129) :: physically take an object (from room or open container), =SaveRoom= for containers, money → gold with optional group auto-split.
- =corpse_consent= (175) :: consent rules for =held_for= corpses and =PK_LOOT= corpses.
- =can_get_unbound_nodrop= (218) :: NODROP pickup gating by =cmd= (=CMD_GET_NODROP=) and owner.
- =CAN_DROP= (740) :: cursed/test/nodrop/insurance/nodrop-content rules for mortals.
- =phys_drop= (763) :: drop with decay timer (=DROP_TICKS=) and auctioneer logging.
- =stealth_penalty= (1349) :: negative modifier from worn metal weight (used by =look.c= inventory peek).
- =power_penalty= (1380) :: negative modifier from worn/carried iron (used by =phys_cast= for power spells).

* obj2.c — eating, drinking, liquids, wearing and removing equipment

** fill (=do_fill=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj2.c:43
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: fill <container> [from] <source>
*** Parameters
- =to_name= :: =one_argument=; must be in =ch->carrying=.
- =from_name= :: =one_argument=; if it abbreviates =from= the next word is used instead. Searched in the room's contents first, then inventory.
*** What it does
Both objects must be =ITEM_DRINKCON=, be different, hold the same liquid type (=value[2]=), the source must not be empty and the target not full. Transfers =min(space in target, source volume)= with =add_contents_object=/=remove_contents_object= (=WATER_DENSITY=) and reports "now full"/"emptied". When an argument is missing the syntax help is printed but the function does not return, so it continues and fails on the object lookup.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =obj->type_flag=, =obj->value[2]=, =obj->contents_volume=, =GET_OBJ_MAX_CONTAINS=.
- Writes :: =to->contents_volume= / weight (via objsize.c), =from->contents_volume=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted with the objects' owner (player save or room save).

** drink (=do_drink=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj2.c:207
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: drink <container>
*** Parameters
- =buf= :: =only_argument=; searched in room contents then inventory.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_DRINK= (85), unused.
*** What it does
Calls =raw_drink(ch,obj,FALSE)=: the object must be =ITEM_DRINKCON=; refused when =DRUNK > 15= while thirsty, =THIRST > 23=, fullness > 22 (clamped to 24), or empty. Less than =DRINK_SIZE= (100) left gives a trickle and empties it. Otherwise =cups = number(3,8)= limited by =24 - THIRST= and by volume; removes =cups*DRINK_SIZE= (vnums 104100/104101 are bottomless). Drinking alcohol on an empty stomach adds +6 =DRUNK=. Applies =drink_aff[type][DRUNK|HUNGER|THIRST|FULLNESS] * cups / 4= via =gain_condition= (a no-op unless the player has =CONFIG_HUNGER=; immortals ≥ =I1= skip fullness) and =gain_fullness=. Poisoned liquid (=value[3]=) joins a =SPELL_POISON= affect lasting =cups*3=. When emptied, containers with max capacity < 2000 are =extract_obj='d, larger ones just reset =value[2]=/=value[3]=.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->conditions[]=, =ch->fullness=, =obj->contents_volume=, =obj->value[2..3]=, =drink_aff[]=, =drinks[]=.
- Writes :: =ch->conditions[]=, =ch->fullness=, =obj->contents_volume=, =obj->value[2..3]=, poison affect, possible object extraction.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only (conditions field 51 and fullness field 110 are written by =save_char= at the next routine save).

** eat (=do_eat=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj2.c:225
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: eat <food>
*** Parameters
- =buf= :: =one_argument=; inventory only.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_EAT= (84), unused.
*** What it does
Must be =ITEM_FOOD= unless level ≥ =I5=. Refused when fullness > 22. In rooms where =!magic_ok= any food carrying an =APPLY_EAT_SPELL= apply with an implemented spell is refused. Adds =value[0]= to =HUNGER= and fullness, then for each =APPLY_EAT_SPELL= apply calls =spell_info[num].spell_pointer(6, ch, "", SPELL_TYPE_POTION, ch, 0, 6)= (unimplemented spells are logged). Poisoned food (=value[3]=) poisons players below =I1= for =value[0]*2=. The food is always extracted.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =temp->type_flag=, =temp->apply[]=, =temp->value[0]=, =temp->value[3]=, =spell_info[]=, =magic_ok(ch->in_room)=.
- Writes :: =ch->conditions[HUNGER]=, =ch->fullness=, spell side effects, poison affect, object extracted.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_URGENT,"Vnum %d: EAT_SPELL %d")= for a missing spell pointer.

** pour (=do_pour=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj2.c:297
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: pour <from> out
: pour <from> <to>
*** Parameters
- =arg1 arg2= :: =argument_interpreter=; both objects must be in =ch->carrying=.
*** What it does
Source must be =ITEM_DRINKCON= and non-empty. =out= removes all contents and resets liquid type and poison. Otherwise the target must be a drink container, empty or holding the same liquid, not full, and not the same object. If the target was empty =name_to_drinkcon= prepends the liquid's =drinknames[]= word to its keyword list. Transfers =min(source volume, free space)=, ORs the poison flag into the target, and resets the source when emptied. Several messages lack a trailing newline.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =obj->type_flag=, =obj->contents_volume=, =VALUE2=/=VALUE3=.
- Writes :: =to_obj->name= (realloc'd), both =contents_volume=, =value[2]=, =value[3]=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted with the player's inventory.

** sip (=do_sip=)

:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: sip <container>
*** Parameters

*** What it does
Drink container, fullness ≤ 23, =DRUNK ≤ 10= ("fail to reach your mouth"), non-empty. Under =DRINK_SIZE= it trickles and empties (except vnums 104100/104101). Applies a quarter of =drink_aff[type]= to =DRUNK=/=HUNGER=/=THIRST=, +1 fullness, removes one =DRINK_SIZE=. Poison (=value[3]=) gives a 2-tick =SPELL_POISON= via =affect_to_char= if not already poisoned. Resets type/poison when emptied but never extracts the container.

- Writes :: =ch->conditions=, =ch->fullness=, =temp->contents_volume=, =temp->value[2..3]=, poison affect.

:END:
*** Syntax
: taste <food|container>
*** Parameters

*** What it does
Fullness ≤ 23. A drink container is delegated to =do_sip=. Otherwise must be =ITEM_FOOD=: +1 =HUNGER=, +1 fullness, poison as in =sip= (2 ticks), and =value[0]--=; when it reaches 0 the food is extracted.

** wear (=do_wear=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj2.c:1114
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: wear <obj>
: wear <obj> <location>
: wear all
*** Parameters
- =arg1= :: =argument_interpreter= first word; =all= or an inventory keyword.
- =arg2= :: optional location, =search_block= partial match against =light finger neck body head legs feet hands arms about waist wield held shield pouch wings trinket= (indices 0..17 = the =wear()= keyword). Unknown → "unknown body location".
*** What it does
=wear all= runs =phys_wear= on every carried object; a named object without location also uses =phys_wear=, which derives the keyword from wear flags (later tests win: trinket > wings > pouch > light type > hold > wield > body > head > about > legs > feet > hands > arms > waist > wrist > neck > finger > shield). =wear()= applies the common gate: mortal Barbarians refuse =IS_MAGIC= items without =REMORT_BARB_NO_JUNK=; =can_have_level_limit=; a unique socket gem already present on any worn item is refused; mortal PCs cannot use =ITEM_CONSTRUCTION= (logged) and nobody uses =ITEM_DAMAGED=. Slot rules: light (0) needs =ITEM_LIGHT=, falls back to hold if the light slot is busy; finger (1) fills L then R; neck (2) fills 1 then 2; body/head/legs/feet/hands/arms/about/waist (3..10) single slots; wrist (11) L then R; wield (12): second weapon requires =SKILL_DUAL_WIELD=, a one-handed shorter weapon, no shield, a free off-hand, and warns if not =ITEM_DUAL_WIELD=; weight limit =str_app[STR].wield_w= below level 2009; two-hander with shield needs =REMORT_TWO_HAND_SPEC=; length-balance checks against the other hand; =ITEM_DUAL_WIELD_ONLY= goes to =HOLD= only when already wielding; otherwise =WIELD= if free else =HOLD=; hold (13): =HOLD= free, weight+2 limit, not two-handed, no shield with a weapon, balance vs wielded weapon; shield (14): no two-hander (unless remort), no held weapon; pouch (15), wings (16), trinket (17, R then L). Success calls =perform_wear= messages, =obj_from_char= and =equip_char=.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =obj->wear_flags=, =obj->extra_flags=, =obj->socket_apply[]=, =ch->equipment[]=, =GET_STR(ch)=, =str_app[]=, =get_skill(ch,SKILL_DUAL_WIELD)=, remort effects, =GET_CLASS(ch)=.
- Writes :: =ch->equipment[pos]= via =equip_char= (which applies affects), =ch->carrying=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, equipment persisted by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_URGENT)= on construction-item use and on an unknown keyword.

** wield, grab, hold (=do_specific_wear=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj2.c:1178
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: wield <weapon>
: hold <obj>
: grab <obj>
*** Parameters
- =buf= :: =only_argument=; must be in =ch->carrying=.
- =cmd= :: selects the =wear()= keyword: =CMD_WIELD= (21) → 12 (wield slot logic, verb "Wield"); =CMD_HOLD= (25) and =CMD_GRAB= (24) → 13 (hold slot, verbs "Hold"/"Grab"). Any other value logs =LOG_URGENT= and returns.
*** What it does
Thin wrapper around =wear(ch,obj,pos)= (see =wear= above for the full slot rules). For hold/grab of an =ITEM_LIGHT= the position is switched to =WEAR_LIGHT= (0), which happens to equal keyword 0 (light), so lights are lit in the light slot instead of held.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, =obj->type_flag=; everything =wear()= reads.
- Writes :: =ch->equipment[WIELD|HOLD|WEAR_LIGHT]=.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_URGENT,"Command %d not done in specific_wear")=.

** remove (=do_remove=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: obj2.c:1327
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: remove <obj>
: remove all
: remove <n>[,<n>...]
*** Parameters
- =arg1= :: =one_argument=. =all=; a comma list of digits (no =.=) indexes the occupied slots in =MAX_WEAR= order (1-based); otherwise an equipment keyword via =get_object_in_equip_vis=.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_REMOVE= (51), unused.
*** What it does
=all= removes each worn item while =CAN_CARRY_OBJ= (immortals always); on the first that would not fit it stops with a message. Numeric and named forms call =phys_remove= even when the item will not fit ("fumble around and try to squeeze"), where =phys_remove= then drops it to the room for mortals. =phys_remove= (below =I9=) refuses cursed items, items whose =last_use_time= is in the future, and items whose item-set ability is on cooldown; then =unequip_char= and =obj_to_char= or =obj_to_room=. Note the numeric index counts occupied slots only, while =equipment= numbers all 22 slots, so the numbers do not match when any slot is empty.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->equipment[]=, =obj->extra_flags=, =obj->last_use_time=, =obj->item_set=, =CAN_CARRY_OBJ=.
- Writes :: =ch->equipment[j]= cleared via =unequip_char=, object to =ch->carrying= or room.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. No direct save; in-memory only, persisted by the next routine player save.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_DEBUG)= on invalid slot / unexpected state in =phys_remove=.

** Helpers in obj2.c
- =name_to_drinkcon= (31) :: prepend the liquid name to a container's keyword list (=name_from_drinkcon= at 15 is unused).
- =raw_drink= (104) :: shared drink logic (also used with =magicDrink=TRUE= elsewhere).
- =perform_wear= (576) / =perform_wear_messages[]= :: per-keyword wear messages.
- =wear= (583) :: the slot-by-slot equip logic driven by keyword 0..17.
- =phys_wear_pos= (1050) :: map a =WEAR_*= position to a keyword and call =wear=.
- =phys_wear= (1084) :: infer keyword from wear flags and call =wear=.
- =phys_wield= (1173) :: =wear(ch,obj,12)=.
- =get_object_in_equip_vis= (1240) :: first visible worn object matching a keyword (with slot index out-param).
- =phys_remove= (1275) :: unequip with curse/cooldown checks and overflow-to-ground.
- =get_rarity= (1410) :: rarity of an object (may raise =obj->rarity= to the prototype's, logging =LOG_MISC=).
- =rarity_color_code= (1436), =obj_color_short_description= (1461) :: rarity-coloured names used throughout.

* look.c — looking, reading, inventory and equipment display

** look (=do_look=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: look.c:913
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: look
: look [at] <target>
: look in <container|char>
: look <direction>
: look room
*** Parameters
- =buf= :: =only_argument=; =at =/=in = prefixes (abbreviation + space) route to =look_at=/=look_in=; a word matching an exit name (=find_exit_name=) routes to =look_dir=; an abbreviation of =room= gives the old-style room view; any other text is =look_at=.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_LOOK= (52), unused (=do_read= passes 15).
*** What it does
Requires a descriptor; refused below sleeping ("stars"), while sleeping, when =AFF_BLIND=, or in a dark room without =CAN_SEE_IN_DARK=. Plain =look= prints the MXP-tagged room name (with "(blue glow)" for =DETECT_MAGIC= in =RELOC_DISTORT|BLUE_GLOW= rooms), the description unless =PLR_BRIEF=, running, or it starts with =HIDDEN=, the exit list unless =PLR_NOEXITS= or room flag =NOSHOWEXIT= (closed/=EX_HIDDEN= exits omitted), a "road leads" summary for =SECT_ROAD= neighbours, an auto-map when =CONFIG_MAP= (=do_map(ch,"auto",CMD_GO)=), room objects (=list_obj_in_room=: identical items stacked, =ITEM_HIDDEN= skipped, traps shown only on a =SKILL_FIND_TRAP= roll), characters (=list_char_in_room=: =AFF_SENSE_LIFE= hints, spell auras) and =show_track=. =look_at= uses =generic_find= over inventory, room, equipment and room characters: a character gets =show_char_to_char= mode 1 (description, health wording, worn equipment) plus mode 2 (inventory) for level ≥ =I5=, and is told "$n looks at you"; a note shows its =action_description=, other objects their matching extra description or "nothing special" plus =obj_condition=; then extra descriptions in the room, worn items, inventory and room objects are searched. =look_in= on a character gives the inventory peek (thieves/bards roll a stat- and level-based check reduced by =stealth_penalty=; level ≥ =I5= sees everything), on a drink container the fullness and liquid colour, on an open container its contents. =look <dir>= prints the exit description and door state, and for immortals =scry_dir= runs =do_at "<to_room> look"=.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =GET_POS(ch)=, =ch->affected_by=, room =name=/=description=/=exits=/=room_flags=/=contents=/=people=/=track[]=, =ch->act= (=PLR_BRIEF=, =PLR_NOEXITS=, =PLR_DEBUG=), =ch->config_bits= (=CONFIG_MAP=, =CONFIG_KEYWORDS=, =CONFIG_SHORT_DESCS=), object =ex_description=, =extra_flags=, condition values, character equipment and affects.
- Writes :: none of its own; =get_rarity= (called while listing) may bump =obj->rarity= to the prototype's.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. Read-only; no state change (apart from the rarity normalisation noted above).
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_MISC,"updated ...")= from =get_rarity=; =vlog(LOG_DEBUG,"XXXXXXXX Look Bug Occured")= if the viewer appears >5 times in the room list.

** read (=do_read=)
:PROPERTIES:

:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: read <obj>
*** Parameters
- =argument= :: whole line; passed on as =at <argument>=.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_READ= (39), unused.
*** What it does
Performs a =generic_find= whose result is discarded, then calls =do_look(ch, "at <argument>", 15)=, so =read= is simply =look at= (notes print their written text).

- Writes :: none.

** equipment (=do_equipment=)

:END:

*** What it does
Lists all =MAX_WEAR= (22) slots numbered 1..22 with the =where[]= label; empty slots print "<nothing>", an object in =HOLD= that is =ITEM_DUAL_WIELD= is labelled as wielded, invisible-to-you objects print "Something.", others go through =show_obj_to_char= mode 1 (short description plus condition tags).

- =obj_condition= (63) :: "(invisible, blue glow, NODROP/owner)" tags plus wear-level wording and "damaged beyond use".
- =appendObjectKeyword= (144) :: append the first keyword when =CONFIG_KEYWORDS= is on.

- =list_char_in_room= (655), =look_at= (678), =look_in= (778), =scry_dir= (824), =look_dir= (842), =look_room= (869) :: the sub-views of =do_look=.

* lockers.c — per-room equipment lockers saved to disk

** list_locker (=do_list_locker=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: lockers.c:41
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:FLAGS: CMDF_NOORDER
:END:
*** Syntax
: list_locker
*** Parameters
- none :: argument ignored.
*** What it does
Needs an object matching "equipment locker" in the room (=generic_find FIND_OBJ_ROOM=). =ReadLocker= extracts that object, loads a fresh locker (vnum 10100) into the room and fills it from the room's locker file; then =do_look(ch,"in locker",0)= lists the contents. Lockers are per room, not per player: every player in the room sees and shares the same contents.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: room contents, =LockerSave/locker.<room vnum>.room=.
- Writes :: room contents (locker object replaced and repopulated).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. Reads =LockerSave/locker.<vnum>.room= (paths are relative to the lib directory, the server's cwd). A malformed record id makes =ReadLocker= call =exit(1)=.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_URGENT)= when the file cannot be opened or the room is missing.

** get_locker (=do_get_locker=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: lockers.c:71
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:FLAGS: CMDF_NOORDER
:END:
*** Syntax
: get_locker <obj>
*** Parameters
- =arg1= :: =only_argument=; =all= is refused; =all.x= and =N*x= prefixes are stripped but only one object is taken (no loop). Empty argument prints the copy-pasted "Put what in the locker?".
*** What it does
Requires the locker object in the room, reloads it with =ReadLocker=, finds the first visible match in =container->contains=. If =can_carry= succeeds, =phys_get= moves it to the player (and calls =SaveRoom=), then =SaveLocker= rewrites the file; otherwise, if =can_shift= allows and the locker is in a room, the object is shifted to the floor with =phys_shift= and the file is rewritten. The local =found= flag is never set (dead).
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: room contents, locker file, =ch->carrying= limits.
- Writes :: locker contents, =ch->carrying= or room floor.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =SaveLocker= → renames =LockerSave/locker.<vnum>.room= to =LockerSave/locker.<vnum>.old= and writes the remaining contents (=WriteObject= per object, terminated by =END_OF_LIST=; the container itself and the =logStr= argument are not written). =phys_get= → =SaveRoom(ch->in_room)= → =RoomSave/<vnum>.room= if the room is =AUTOSAVE=.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_URGENT)= on rename/open failures (backup restored on write failure).

** put_locker (=do_put_locker=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: lockers.c:140
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:FLAGS: CMDF_NOORDER
:END:
*** Syntax
: put_locker <obj>
*** Parameters
- =arg1= :: =only_argument=; inventory object via =generic_find FIND_OBJ_INV=; =all= refused.
*** What it does
Requires the locker in the room. An =ITEM_NODROP= object is refused: bound to the player → "It's bound to you!", otherwise a =LOG_BUGS= line (note =str_cmp= is called on =obj_object->owner=, which may be NULL for unbound items). =ReadLocker= reloads the locker and both objects are looked up again. Cursed items are refused. Mortals: a limited item (prototype =max_exist < 32000=), a container with anything inside, or "rubbish" (=cost_per_day < 0=, =ITEM_TRASH=, =ITEM_CONSTRUCTION=, =ITEM_ARENA_ONLY=, =ITEM_TEST=, =ITEM_NODROP=, =is_no_rent=) is refused and dropped on the floor "in disgust". Insurance token (25) and corpses refused; locker must be open. Capacity is the odd test =GET_OBJ_WEIGHT(container) < container->cost=. On success the object moves into the locker, =SaveRoom= runs, and =SaveLocker= writes the file.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =ch->carrying=, =obj->extra_flags=, =obj->wear_flags=, =obj->cost_per_day=, =real_objp(vnum)->max_exist=, =container->value[1]=, =container->cost=.
- Writes :: locker contents, =ch->carrying=, possibly the room floor.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: =SaveLocker= → =LockerSave/locker.<room vnum>.room= (backup =.old=); =SaveRoom(ch->in_room)= → =RoomSave/<vnum>.room= for =AUTOSAVE= rooms.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_BUGS,"%s has gotten a hold of %s owned by %s...")=; =LOG_URGENT= on file errors.

** log_locker (=do_log_locker=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: lockers.c:300
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:FLAGS: CMDF_NOORDER
:END:
*** Syntax
: log_locker
*** Parameters
- none :: ignored.
*** What it does
Empty function body: the command is registered but does nothing and prints nothing. The =logStr= built by =get_locker=/=put_locker= (timestamp, player, object name and description) is passed to =SaveLocker= but never written anywhere, so there is no locker log.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: none.
- Writes :: none.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. Read-only; no state change.

** Helpers in lockers.c
- =SaveLocker= (305) :: rotate =LockerSave/locker.<vnum>.room= → =.old= and write the locker's contents (=get_rarity= is forced on each object first).
- =ReadLocker= (359) :: extract the room's locker object, create vnum 10100 anew, read the file's =OBJECT= records into it; =exit(1)= on an unknown record id.

* clan.c — clan membership and the clan channel

Clan definitions (=clan_info=: name, flags, xp_mult, min/max_join_level, clanmaster, deputy) are loaded read-only from =clans.out= in the lib directory by =boot_clans= (db.c:98 at boot, and =reload clan= in wizard.c). Nothing in clan.c or clandb.c writes that file. A player's membership is =ch->clan= (player-file field 66) and =ch->clan_rank= (field 120), both written by =save_char=; =ch->clan_invite= / =ch->clan_invite_rank= are in-memory only and vanish on logout. Clans 1 and 2 are the default -PK and +PK "no clan" clans (=ch->clan <= 2= means unaffiliated).

** clanlist, clist (=do_clan_list=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: clan.c:62
:MIN_POS: RES (resting)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: clanlist
*** Parameters
- none :: ignored.
*** What it does
Prints one line per defined clan: index, PK flags (=+PKA= both, =+PKO= =CLAN_PKOTHERS=, =+PKC= =CLAN_PKCLAN=, =-PK=), =+OPEN=/=-OPEN= (=CLAN_OPENENROLL=) and name. =CLAN_PRIVATE= clans are hidden unless the viewer is a member or immortal. The loop runs to =MAX_CLANS= inclusive but =get_clan_with_index= bounds-checks.

- Writes :: none.

:PROPERTIES:

*** Syntax
: clanjoin [+pka|+pko|+pkc|-pk] <clan name>
: clanjoin
*** Parameters

*** What it does
No argument prints help. The clan must exist and be =CLAN_OPENENROLL= or exactly the name stored in =ch->clan_invite=. If a flag was typed it must equal the clan's =clan_flags= string (for -PK clans only =-pk= is accepted). Level must lie within =min_join_level=..=max_join_level= when set. A member of a =CLAN_PKOTHERS= clan cannot join a non-PKOTHERS clan. On success the invite is freed, =ch->clan = index=, =ch->clan_rank = ch->clan_invite_rank= (0 for open enrollment) and the rank description is echoed.

** claninvite (=do_clan_invite=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: clan.c:190
:MIN_POS: STA (standing)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:FLAGS: CMDF_NOORDER
:END:
*** Syntax
: claninvite <player> [Member|Officer|Leader|Owner]
*** Parameters
- =player= :: =one_argument=; =get_char_room_vis=, must be a connected PC.
- =rank= :: optional =search_block= over =clan_ranks[]=; defaults to =CLAN_RANK_MEMBER=; clamped 0..3.
*** What it does
Intended for officers and above (=get_clan_rank(ch) >= CLAN_RANK_OFFICER=), but the check only prints a message and does not return, so any member can invite. Refuses mobs, link-dead players, targets already holding another clan's invite, +PK members being invited to a -PK clan, and level outside the clan's bounds (the "too low" test mistakenly compares against =max_join_level=). The offered rank must be below the inviter's unless the inviter is the owner; same-clan targets of equal/higher rank are refused. Sets =v->clan_invite = strdup(clan name)= and =v->clan_invite_rank=, then tells the target the clan's PK/stealth/private implications and how to =clanjoin=/=clanreject=.

*** Persistence

- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_IMP)= from =get_clan_rank= on an out-of-range rank.

The target is moved to clan 2 (if the clan is =CLAN_PKOTHERS=) or 1; =v->clan_rank= is left unchanged.

* help.c — help file lookup

** help (=do_help=)
:PROPERTIES:
:FILE: help.c:250
:MIN_POS: DEA (dead)
:MIN_LEVEL: 0 (mortal)
:END:
*** Syntax
: help
: help <keyword>
*** Parameters
- =argument= :: rest of line after leading whitespace; compared with =str_cmp= (exact) and =is_abbrev= (best abbreviation retained as fallback) against the sorted =help_index[]=.
- =cmd= :: =CMD_HELP= (91), unused.
*** What it does
Requires a descriptor. With no keyword sends the global =help= page (loaded at boot from lib file =help=, =HELP_PAGE_FILE=). Otherwise binary-searches =help_index= (built from =help_table=, =HELP_KWRD_FILE=, by =build_help_index=); no match logs =LOG_HELP= and says so, and an entry whose =min_level= exceeds the player's is hidden the same way. Then seeks =help_fl= to the entry and assembles it: old format ends at =#=, honours =!>N=/=!<N= (stop unless level ≥/≤ N) and =>N:=/=<N:= conditional lines; new (=.newhelp=) format prints until =.begin=/=.end=, renders =.usage= and =.xrefs= sections indented with the configured colour codes, applies =.minlevel= gates, skips =.absminlev=/=.topics=, and bolds the first line. Output goes through =page_string=. Entries longer than =MAX_STRING_LENGTH= are aborted and logged.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =help=, =help_index[]=, =top_of_helpt=, =help_fl= (file position), =newhelp=, =usage_code=/=xrefs_code=/=text_code=, =GET_LEVEL(ch)=.
- Writes :: none (moves the shared =help_fl= file offset).
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. Read-only; no state change.
- Log lines: =vlog(LOG_HELP,"%s searched for: %s (no HELP entry found)")=; =vlog(LOG_URGENT)= on over-long entries or malformed =>=/=<= lines.

:END:
*** Syntax
: listhelp

: listhelp <topic>
*** Parameters
- =temp= :: =one_word=; =all=, empty, or an abbreviation of a topic from the =.topics= header.
*** What it does
NPCs are ignored. With the old help format, or =all=, lists every keyword the player's level may see, 7 per line truncated to 10 characters. With no argument lists the topic names (5 per line). Otherwise finds the first topic matching the abbreviation and lists its level-filtered keywords; an unknown topic falls back to the topic list.
*** Data touched
- Reads  :: =newhelp=, =help_index[]=, =topic_list=, =GET_LEVEL(ch)=.
- Writes :: none.
*** Persistence
- Direct saves: none. Read-only; no state change.

- =build_topic_list= (34), =enter_topics= (72), =free_topic_list= (16) :: the topic → keyword lists used by =listhelp=.
